Why loops<\/h3><p>A loop repeats a block of code. Use <b>for<\/b> when you know the count, <b>while<\/b> when you repeat until a condition changes, and <b>do-while<\/b> when the body must run at least once (menus, input validation).<\/p>\n<h3>2. The three loops<\/h3><pre class='cd'>for (int i = 1; i &lt;= 5; i++) {\n    System.out.println(i);          // 1 2 3 4 5\n}\n\nint n = 5;\nwhile (n &gt; 0) {                    // condition checked FIRST\n    System.out.println(n);\n    n--;                            // forgetting this = infinite loop\n}\n\nint x;\ndo {\n    x = sc.nextInt();               // body runs once, THEN condition is checked\n} while (x &lt; 0);<\/pre><ul><li>A for loop has three parts: <b>init; condition; update<\/b>. Init runs once, the condition is checked before every round, the update runs after every round.<\/li><li>for-each (<b>for (int v : arr)<\/b>) reads every element of an array or collection; you will use it from the Arrays topic onward.<\/li><\/ul>\n<h3>3. break, continue, labels<\/h3><pre class='cd'>for (int i = 1; i &lt;= 10; i++) {\n    if (i == 3) continue;   // skip 3\n    if (i == 6) break;      // stop the loop completely\n    System.out.print(i + \" \");   // 1 2 4 5\n}\n\nouter:\nfor (int i = 1; i &lt;= 3; i++)\n    for (int j = 1; j &lt;= 3; j++) {\n        if (i * j == 4) break outer;   // leaves BOTH loops\n    }<\/pre><p>A plain <b>break<\/b> leaves only the nearest loop. <b>continue<\/b> jumps to the update step of a for loop.<\/p>\n<h3>4. Digit tricks (very common in tests)<\/h3><pre class='cd'>int n = 1234, sum = 0, rev = 0;\nwhile (n &gt; 0) {\n    int d = n % 10;        // last digit\n    sum += d;              // digit sum\n    rev = rev * 10 + d;    // reverse number\n    n /= 10;               // drop last digit\n}\n// sum = 10, rev = 4321<\/pre><p>Palindrome number: keep a copy of the original and compare it with <b>rev<\/b>. Armstrong number: sum of each digit raised to the number of digits equals the number (153 = 1&sup3; + 5&sup3; + 3&sup3;).<\/p>\n<h3>5. Prime, factorial, Fibonacci<\/h3><pre class='cd'>// Prime check: only test up to sqrt(n)\nboolean prime = n &gt; 1;\nfor (int i = 2; i * i &lt;= n; i++)\n    if (n % i == 0) { prime = false; break; }\n\n// Factorial (use long, 20! is the limit)\nlong f = 1;\nfor (int i = 2; i &lt;= n; i++) f *= i;\n\n// Fibonacci: 0 1 1 2 3 5 8 ...\nint a = 0, b = 1;\nfor (int i = 0; i &lt; n; i++) {\n    System.out.print(a + \" \");\n    int next = a + b; a = b; b = next;\n}<\/pre><p><b>Why sqrt(n)?<\/b> Factors come in pairs (a, b) with a &times; b = n. One of the two is always at most sqrt(n), so if there is no factor up to sqrt(n) there is none at all.<\/p>\n<h3>6. Pattern printing method<\/h3><p>Outer loop = rows. Inner loop = columns. For each row ask: how many spaces, how many symbols, as a formula of the row number <b>i<\/b>.<\/p><pre class='cd'>// Right triangle (n = 4)\nfor (int i = 1; i &lt;= n; i++) {\n    for (int j = 1; j &lt;= i; j++) System.out.print(\"* \");\n    System.out.println();\n}\n// Pyramid: (n - i) spaces, then (2*i - 1) stars\nfor (int i = 1; i &lt;= n; i++) {\n    for (int s = 1; s &lt;= n - i; s++) System.out.print(\" \");\n    for (int k = 1; k &lt;= 2 * i - 1; k++) System.out.print(\"*\");\n    System.out.println();\n}<\/pre><table><tr><th>Pattern<\/th><th>Spaces in row i<\/th><th>Symbols in row i<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Right triangle<\/td><td>0<\/td><td>i<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Inverted triangle<\/td><td>0<\/td><td>n - i + 1<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Pyramid<\/td><td>n - i<\/td><td>2i - 1<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>7. Trace table (dry run)<\/h3><p>Before running code, write the variable values after every round. Example: <b>for (i=1; i&lt;=3; i++) sum+=i<\/b><\/p><table><tr><th>Round<\/th><th>i<\/th><th>sum<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>1<\/td><td>1<\/td><td>1<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>2<\/td><td>2<\/td><td>3<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>3<\/td><td>3<\/td><td>6<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>8. Common mistakes<\/h3><ul><li>Off-by-one: using &lt; instead of &lt;= (or the reverse).<\/li><li>Forgetting the update, giving an infinite loop.<\/li><li>A semicolon right after the for(...) header: <b>for(...);<\/b> makes the body empty.<\/li><li>Using int for factorial and overflowing past 12!.<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\nFN[\"0_3\"]=`<h3>1. What is a method<\/h3><p>A method is a named block of code you can call many times. It gives reuse, readability and easier testing.<\/p><pre class='cd'>static int add(int a, int b) {     // return type, name, parameters\n    return a + b;\n}\nint r = add(3, 4);                 // call: r = 7<\/pre><ul><li><b>Parameters<\/b> are the variables in the definition; <b>arguments<\/b> are the values you pass in the call.<\/li><li>A <b>void<\/b> method returns nothing. Any other return type needs a <b>return<\/b> on every path.<\/li><li>Methods that main calls directly are marked <b>static<\/b> for now; instance methods come with classes.<\/li><\/ul>\n<h3>2. Pass by value<\/h3><p>Java <b>always<\/b> copies the argument.<\/p><pre class='cd'>static void change(int x) { x = 99; }\nint a = 5; change(a);\nSystem.out.println(a);          // still 5\n\nstatic void rename(StringBuilder sb) { sb.append(\"!\"); }   // changes the object\nstatic void reset(StringBuilder sb)  { sb = new StringBuilder(\"new\"); } // does NOT affect caller<\/pre><p>For objects, the <b>reference<\/b> is copied. Both the caller and the method point to the same object, so changing its contents is visible. Pointing the copy somewhere else is not.<\/p>\n<h3>3. Overloading<\/h3><pre class='cd'>static double area(double r)           { return Math.PI * r * r; }\nstatic double area(double l, double b) { return l * b; }\nstatic double area(int s)              { return s * s; }<\/pre><ul><li>Same name, <b>different parameter list<\/b> (type, number or order).<\/li><li>Changing only the return type is a compile error.<\/li><li>The compiler picks the method by argument types at compile time, so overloading is <b>compile-time polymorphism<\/b>.<\/li><\/ul>\n<h3>4. Scope<\/h3><pre class='cd'>for (int i = 0; i &lt; 3; i++) { int sq = i * i; }\n// i and sq do not exist here: block scope<\/pre><p>A local variable exists only inside its block and must be assigned before use. A field (declared in the class) lives with the object or class.<\/p>\n<h3>5. Recursion<\/h3><p>A method that calls itself. It needs a <b>base case<\/b> (stops) and a <b>smaller problem<\/b> (moves toward the base case).<\/p><pre class='cd'>static long fact(int n) {\n    if (n &lt;= 1) return 1;          // base case\n    return n * fact(n - 1);        // smaller problem\n}\n// fact(4) = 4 * fact(3) = 4 * 3 * fact(2) = 4 * 3 * 2 * fact(1) = 24<\/pre><table><tr><th>Call stack for fact(4)<\/th><th>Waiting for<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>fact(4)<\/td><td>4 * fact(3)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>fact(3)<\/td><td>3 * fact(2)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>fact(2)<\/td><td>2 * fact(1)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>fact(1)<\/td><td>returns 1, then the stack unwinds<\/td><\/tr><\/table><p>No base case or never reaching it gives <b>StackOverflowError<\/b>. Recursion costs stack memory; a loop is cheaper for simple repetition.<\/p>\n<h3>6. Utility methods to write<\/h3><pre class='cd'>static boolean isPrime(int n) {\n    if (n &lt; 2) return false;\n    for (int i = 2; i * i &lt;= n; i++) if (n % i == 0) return false;\n    return true;\n}\nstatic int gcd(int a, int b) { return b == 0 ? a : gcd(b, a % b); }   // Euclid\nstatic long power(int b, int e) { return e == 0 ? 1 : b * power(b, e - 1); }<\/pre>\n<h3>7. Common mistakes<\/h3><ul><li>Missing return on one branch: \"missing return statement\".<\/li><li>Expecting a swap(int, int) method to swap the caller's variables.<\/li><li>Two methods differing only in return type.<\/li><li>Recursion with a base case that is never reached.<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\nFN[\"0_4\"]=`<h3>1. Array basics<\/h3><p>An array stores a fixed number of values of one type in a row of memory. Indexes start at <b>0<\/b>; the last index is <b>length - 1<\/b>.<\/p><pre class='cd'>int[] a = new int[5];            // {0,0,0,0,0}\nint[] b = {4, 2, 9, 7};          // length 4\na[0] = 10;\nSystem.out.println(b.length);    // 4   (field, no brackets)\n// b[4] throws ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException<\/pre><p>Defaults: 0 for numbers, false for boolean, null for objects such as String.<\/p>\n<h3>2. Traversal<\/h3><pre class='cd'>for (int i = 0; i &lt; b.length; i++) System.out.print(b[i] + \" \");\nfor (int v : b) System.out.print(v + \" \");   // read-only style<\/pre>\n<h3>3. Core algorithms<\/h3><pre class='cd'>// Max and second max in one pass\nint max = Integer.MIN_VALUE, second = Integer.MIN_VALUE;\nfor (int v : b) {\n    if (v &gt; max) { second = max; max = v; }\n    else if (v &gt; second &amp;&amp; v != max) second = v;\n}\n\n// Reverse in place (two pointers)\nfor (int i = 0, j = b.length - 1; i &lt; j; i++, j--) {\n    int t = b[i]; b[i] = b[j]; b[j] = t;\n}\n\n// Move zeros to end\nint pos = 0;\nfor (int i = 0; i &lt; b.length; i++) if (b[i] != 0) b[pos++] = b[i];\nwhile (pos &lt; b.length) b[pos++] = 0;<\/pre><p>Rotate right by k: reverse the whole array, reverse the first k, reverse the rest (use k % n first).<\/p>\n<h3>4. The Arrays class<\/h3><table><tr><th>Method<\/th><th>Use<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Arrays.toString(a)<\/td><td>Readable print of a 1-D array<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Arrays.deepToString(m)<\/td><td>Readable print of a 2-D array<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Arrays.sort(a)<\/td><td>Sort in place<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Arrays.fill(a, 7)<\/td><td>Set every element<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Arrays.copyOf(a, n)<\/td><td>Copy to a new array of length n<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Arrays.equals(a, b)<\/td><td>Compare contents<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Arrays.binarySearch(a, k)<\/td><td>Search a SORTED array<\/td><\/tr><\/table><p><b>b = a<\/b> copies only the reference; both names then point to the same array. Use <b>a.clone()<\/b> or <b>Arrays.copyOf<\/b> for a real copy.<\/p>\n<h3>5. 2-D arrays<\/h3><pre class='cd'>int[][] m = new int[3][4];                 // 3 rows, 4 columns, 12 cells\nint[][] g = {{1,2,3},{4,5,6}};             // 2 x 3\nfor (int i = 0; i &lt; g.length; i++)         // rows\n    for (int j = 0; j &lt; g[i].length; j++)  // columns\n        System.out.print(g[i][j] + \" \");\n\n// Transpose of a 2 x 3 matrix\nint[][] t = new int[3][2];\nfor (int i = 0; i &lt; 2; i++)\n    for (int j = 0; j &lt; 3; j++) t[j][i] = g[i][j];<\/pre><p>Row sum: add g[i][j] over j. Column sum: add g[i][j] over i. Main diagonal: cells where i == j. A <b>jagged<\/b> array has rows of different lengths: <b>new int[3][]<\/b>, then create each row.<\/p>\n<h3>6. Frequency array trick<\/h3><pre class='cd'>int[] freq = new int[26];\nfor (char c : \"banana\".toCharArray()) freq[c - 'a']++;\n// freq['a'-'a'] = 3, freq['b'-'a'] = 1, freq['n'-'a'] = 2<\/pre><p>This counts letters in O(n) with no HashMap; it is the base of the anagram check.<\/p>\n<h3>7. Common mistakes<\/h3><ul><li>Loop condition <b>i &lt;= a.length<\/b> causes an out-of-range error.<\/li><li>Printing an array directly shows something like [I@1b6d3586.<\/li><li>Using .length() instead of .length for arrays.<\/li><li>Copying with = and then being surprised that both change.<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\n/* PACK M1-B2: paste ABOVE the line  draw();  (after batch 1). Completes all Month 1 lessons. */\n\nFN[\"0_5\"]=`<h3>1. Strings are immutable<\/h3><p>A String object never changes. Methods like <b>toUpperCase()<\/b> return a NEW String. Literals are shared in the <b>string pool<\/b>.<\/p><pre class='cd'>String a = \"hi\", b = \"hi\";\nString c = new String(\"hi\");\na == b          // true  (same pooled object)\na == c          // false (different objects)\na.equals(c)     // true  (same characters)<\/pre><p>Always compare content with <b>equals<\/b> (or <b>equalsIgnoreCase<\/b>); <b>compareTo<\/b> gives ordering (0 if equal).<\/p>\n<h3>2. Methods to know<\/h3><table><tr><th>Method<\/th><th>Example on \"Java\"<\/th><th>Result<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>length()<\/td><td>s.length()<\/td><td>4<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>charAt(i)<\/td><td>s.charAt(1)<\/td><td>'a'<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>substring(b, e)<\/td><td>s.substring(1,3)<\/td><td>\"av\" (end excluded)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>indexOf(x)<\/td><td>s.indexOf(\"v\")<\/td><td>2 (-1 if absent)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>replace(a, b)<\/td><td>s.replace('a','o')<\/td><td>\"Jovo\"<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>split(\",\")<\/td><td>\"a,b,c\".split(\",\")<\/td><td>array of 3<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>trim()<\/td><td>\"  x \".trim()<\/td><td>\"x\"<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>toCharArray()<\/td><td>s.toCharArray()<\/td><td>char[]<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>3. StringBuilder<\/h3><pre class='cd'>StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder();\nfor (int i = 0; i &lt; 5; i++) sb.append(i).append(',');\nsb.reverse();  sb.insert(0, \"X\");  sb.deleteCharAt(sb.length() - 1);\nString out = sb.toString();<\/pre><p>Use it inside loops: each <b>+=<\/b> on a String creates a new object (O(n&sup2;) overall). StringBuffer is the thread-safe, slower twin.<\/p>\n<h3>4. Worked solutions<\/h3><pre class='cd'>// Palindrome\nboolean pal = new StringBuilder(s).reverse().toString().equals(s);\n\n// Anagram check\nchar[] x = s1.toCharArray(), y = s2.toCharArray();\nArrays.sort(x); Arrays.sort(y);\nboolean ana = Arrays.equals(x, y);\n\n// Vowel count\nint v = 0;\nfor (char ch : s.toLowerCase().toCharArray())\n    if (\"aeiou\".indexOf(ch) != -1) v++;<\/pre>\n<h3>5. Common mistakes<\/h3><ul><li>Using == for content.<\/li><li>Ignoring the returned value: <b>s.toUpperCase();<\/b> alone does nothing.<\/li><li>substring end index is exclusive.<\/li><li>charAt out of range throws StringIndexOutOfBoundsException.<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\nFN[\"0_6\"]=`<h3>1. Class and object<\/h3><p>A <b>class<\/b> is a blueprint (fields + methods). An <b>object<\/b> is one instance built from it with <b>new<\/b>; it lives on the heap, and the variable holds a reference.<\/p><pre class='cd'>class Student {\n    int id; String name; double marks;          // fields (state)\n    Student(int id, String name, double marks) { // constructor\n        this.id = id; this.name = name; this.marks = marks;\n    }\n    void display() { System.out.println(id + \" \" + name + \" \" + marks); }  // behaviour\n}\nStudent s1 = new Student(1, \"Asha\", 82.5);\ns1.display();<\/pre>\n<h3>2. Constructors<\/h3><ul><li>Same name as the class, no return type.<\/li><li>If you write none, Java gives a default no-argument one. Write any constructor and the default disappears.<\/li><li>Overload constructors and chain with <b>this(...)<\/b> (must be the first line).<\/li><\/ul><pre class='cd'>Student() { this(0, \"Unknown\", 0); }   // chaining<\/pre>\n<h3>3. this<\/h3><p><b>this<\/b> is the current object. It separates a field from a same-named parameter (<b>this.name = name<\/b>) and passes the current object to another method.<\/p>\n<h3>4. static<\/h3><pre class='cd'>class Counter {\n    static int count = 0;       // ONE copy shared by all objects\n    Counter() { count++; }\n    static int getCount() { return count; }   // no this inside\n}\nnew Counter(); new Counter();\nCounter.getCount();   // 2<\/pre><p>Static members belong to the class and are called as <b>ClassName.member<\/b>. A static method cannot directly use instance fields. A <b>static block<\/b> runs once when the class loads.<\/p>\n<h3>5. Initialisation order<\/h3><table><tr><th>Step<\/th><th>What runs<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>1<\/td><td>static blocks (once, at class load)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>2<\/td><td>instance blocks / field initialisers<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>3<\/td><td>constructor body<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>6. Common mistakes<\/h3><ul><li>Calling new Student() after writing only a parameterised constructor.<\/li><li>Writing <b>name = name<\/b> in a constructor, which assigns the parameter to itself.<\/li><li>Using an instance field from main without creating an object.<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\nFN[\"0_7\"]=`<h3>1. Encapsulation<\/h3><p>Hide the fields (<b>private<\/b>) and expose controlled access through public methods. The class then guarantees its own data is always valid.<\/p><pre class='cd'>class Employee {\n    private String name;\n    private double salary;\n    public double getSalary() { return salary; }\n    public void setSalary(double s) {\n        if (s &lt; 0) throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"Salary cannot be negative\");\n        this.salary = s;\n    }\n}<\/pre>\n<h3>2. Access modifiers<\/h3><table><tr><th>Modifier<\/th><th>Same class<\/th><th>Same package<\/th><th>Subclass (other package)<\/th><th>Everywhere<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>private<\/td><td>Yes<\/td><td>No<\/td><td>No<\/td><td>No<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>(default)<\/td><td>Yes<\/td><td>Yes<\/td><td>No<\/td><td>No<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>protected<\/td><td>Yes<\/td><td>Yes<\/td><td>Yes<\/td><td>No<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>public<\/td><td>Yes<\/td><td>Yes<\/td><td>Yes<\/td><td>Yes<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>3. Packages<\/h3><pre class='cd'>package com.bank.model;       // first line of the file\nimport com.bank.service.AccountService;<\/pre><p>Packages group related classes and avoid name clashes. Names are lowercase; folder structure matches the package name.<\/p>\n<h3>4. Immutable class<\/h3><pre class='cd'>public final class Point {\n    private final int x, y;\n    public Point(int x, int y) { this.x = x; this.y = y; }\n    public int getX() { return x; }\n    public Point withX(int nx) { return new Point(nx, y); }   // returns a new object\n}<\/pre><p>Final class, private final fields, no setters. Safe to share between threads. String is the classic example.<\/p>\n<h3>5. Common mistakes<\/h3><ul><li>Public fields that anyone can corrupt.<\/li><li>Getter returning a mutable array or list directly (callers can change it); return a copy.<\/li><li>Setter with no validation.<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\nFN[\"0_8\"]=`<h3>1. Inheritance<\/h3><p><b>extends<\/b> gives a subclass the parent's fields and methods (IS-A). Java classes have <b>single<\/b> inheritance; every class ultimately extends <b>Object<\/b>.<\/p><pre class='cd'>class Vehicle {\n    protected String brand;\n    Vehicle(String brand) { this.brand = brand; }\n    void describe() { System.out.println(\"Vehicle: \" + brand); }\n}\nclass Car extends Vehicle {\n    int doors;\n    Car(String brand, int doors) { super(brand); this.doors = doors; }  // super first\n    @Override void describe() { super.describe(); System.out.println(\"Doors: \" + doors); }\n}<\/pre>\n<h3>2. super<\/h3><ul><li><b>super(...)<\/b>: calls the parent constructor; must be the first statement.<\/li><li><b>super.method()<\/b>: calls the parent's version.<\/li><li>Constructor order: parent runs first, then child (Grandparent, Parent, Child).<\/li><\/ul>\n<h3>3. Overriding rules<\/h3><ul><li>Same name and parameters as the parent method.<\/li><li>Access may stay the same or widen, never narrow.<\/li><li>Return type same or a subtype.<\/li><li>Use <b>@Override<\/b> so the compiler checks it.<\/li><li>private, static and final methods cannot be overridden.<\/li><\/ul>\n<h3>4. Overloading vs overriding<\/h3><table><tr><th><\/th><th>Overloading<\/th><th>Overriding<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Where<\/td><td>Same class<\/td><td>Parent and child<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Signature<\/td><td>Must differ<\/td><td>Must match<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Resolved<\/td><td>Compile time<\/td><td>Runtime<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>5. Object class and final<\/h3><p>Override <b>toString()<\/b> for readable printing, and <b>equals()<\/b> with <b>hashCode()<\/b> for content comparison. <b>final<\/b> on a variable = constant, on a method = no overriding, on a class = no subclasses.<\/p>\n<h3>6. Common mistakes<\/h3><ul><li>No matching parent constructor: the child must call super(args) explicitly.<\/li><li>Using inheritance for HAS-A relations; use a field instead (a Car HAS an Engine).<\/li><li>Overriding equals but not hashCode.<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\nFN[\"0_9\"]=`<h3>1. Polymorphism<\/h3><p>One reference type, many behaviours. <b>Compile-time<\/b> = overloading. <b>Runtime<\/b> = overriding: the JVM runs the method of the <b>actual object<\/b>, not the reference type (dynamic dispatch).<\/p><pre class='cd'>Shape s = new Circle(2);   // upcasting\ns.area();                  // runs Circle's area()\nShape[] all = { new Circle(2), new Square(3) };\nfor (Shape x : all) System.out.println(x.area());<\/pre>\n<h3>2. Casting<\/h3><pre class='cd'>Shape s = new Circle(2);\nif (s instanceof Circle) {\n    Circle c = (Circle) s;   // downcast, checked first\n    c.radiusOnlyMethod();\n}<\/pre><p>Upcasting is automatic and safe; through the parent reference only parent members are visible. A wrong downcast throws <b>ClassCastException<\/b>.<\/p>\n<h3>3. Abstract class<\/h3><pre class='cd'>abstract class Shape {\n    String color;\n    abstract double area();            // no body: subclasses must implement\n    void show() { System.out.println(color + \" \" + area()); }   // concrete\n}\nclass Circle extends Shape {\n    double r;\n    Circle(double r) { this.r = r; }\n    double area() { return Math.PI * r * r; }\n}<\/pre><p>Cannot be instantiated; may have fields, constructors and both kinds of methods.<\/p>\n<h3>4. Interface<\/h3><pre class='cd'>interface Payment {\n    void pay(double amount);\n    default void receipt() { System.out.println(\"Paid\"); }   // Java 8+\n}\nclass UPI implements Payment { public void pay(double a) { System.out.println(\"UPI \" + a); } }\nclass Card implements Payment { public void pay(double a) { System.out.println(\"Card \" + a); } }\nvoid checkout(Payment p) { p.pay(500); }     // works for any Payment<\/pre><p>A class may implement many interfaces. Interface fields are implicitly <b>public static final<\/b>; methods are public.<\/p>\n<h3>5. Abstract class vs interface<\/h3><table><tr><th><\/th><th>Abstract class<\/th><th>Interface<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Inherit from<\/td><td>One<\/td><td>Many<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>State (fields)<\/td><td>Yes<\/td><td>Constants only<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Constructors<\/td><td>Yes<\/td><td>No<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Use for<\/td><td>Shared code and state (IS-A)<\/td><td>A capability or contract (CAN-DO)<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>6. Common mistakes<\/h3><ul><li>Expecting field access to be polymorphic; only methods are.<\/li><li>Downcasting without instanceof.<\/li><li>Forgetting public when implementing an interface method.<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\nFN[\"0_10\"]=`<h3>Project: Console ATM<\/h3><p>This is a two-day build. Day 1 gets a working menu, Day 2 makes it solid and publishable.<\/p>\n<h3>1. Requirements<\/h3><ul><li>Check balance, deposit, withdraw, change PIN, mini statement, exit.<\/li><li>PIN check with 3 attempts. Reject negative or non-numeric input without crashing.<\/li><\/ul>\n<h3>2. Class design<\/h3><table><tr><th>Class<\/th><th>Responsibility<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Account<\/td><td>private balance, pin; deposit(), withdraw(), verifyPin(), history list<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>ATM<\/td><td>menu loop, reads input, calls Account<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Main<\/td><td>creates an Account and starts the ATM<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>3. Skeleton<\/h3><pre class='cd'>class Account {\n    private double balance; private int pin;\n    private final java.util.List&lt;String&gt; history = new java.util.ArrayList&lt;&gt;();\n    Account(int pin, double opening) { this.pin = pin; this.balance = opening; }\n    boolean verifyPin(int p) { return p == pin; }\n    void deposit(double a) {\n        if (a &lt;= 0) throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"Amount must be positive\");\n        balance += a; history.add(\"Deposit \" + a);\n    }\n    void withdraw(double a) {\n        if (a &lt;= 0 || a &gt; balance) throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"Invalid or insufficient\");\n        balance -= a; history.add(\"Withdraw \" + a);\n    }\n    double getBalance() { return balance; }\n}<\/pre><pre class='cd'>// Menu loop in ATM\nint choice;\ndo {\n    System.out.println(\"1 Balance  2 Deposit  3 Withdraw  4 Exit\");\n    choice = Integer.parseInt(sc.nextLine());\n    switch (choice) {\n        case 1: System.out.println(acc.getBalance()); break;\n        case 2: acc.deposit(Double.parseDouble(sc.nextLine())); break;\n        case 3: acc.withdraw(Double.parseDouble(sc.nextLine())); break;\n    }\n} while (choice != 4);<\/pre><p>Read lines with nextLine and parse them; this avoids the nextInt newline problem. Wrap the body in try/catch for NumberFormatException and IllegalArgumentException.<\/p>\n<h3>4. Day 2 checklist<\/h3><ul><li>PIN attempts counter in a loop.<\/li><li>Mini statement: print the last 5 history entries.<\/li><li>README: what it does, how to compile and run, a sample session.<\/li><li>Be ready to explain your class design in 2 minutes.<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\nFN[\"0_11\"]=`<h3>Month 1 review<\/h3><p>Day 1 is attempt, Day 2 is repair. Aim for honest scoring.<\/p>\n<h3>1. Attempt rules<\/h3><ul><li>15 HackerRank Java problems (Basics, Strings, OOP), 20 minutes max each, no hints.<\/li><li>Keep a log: <b>problem | what went wrong | concept<\/b>.<\/li><\/ul>\n<h3>2. Checklist to self-test (answer aloud)<\/h3><table><tr><th>Area<\/th><th>Can you explain?<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Basics<\/td><td>JDK vs JRE vs JVM; widening vs narrowing; integer division<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Control flow<\/td><td>for vs while vs do-while; break vs continue; why prime check stops at sqrt(n)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Methods<\/td><td>pass by value; overloading rules; recursion base case<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Arrays and Strings<\/td><td>default values; == vs equals; String vs StringBuilder<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>OOP<\/td><td>encapsulation, inheritance, polymorphism, abstraction with your own code<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>3. Repair plan<\/h3><ul><li>Redo every wrong problem from scratch.<\/li><li>For each weak concept, write one fresh example in your own words.<\/li><li>Record yourself explaining the four OOP pillars and listen for gaps.<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\n/* PACK M2-LESSONS: paste ABOVE the line  draw();  (after the Month 1 packs). */\n\nFN[\"1_0\"]=`<h3>1. Exception hierarchy<\/h3><p>Throwable has two branches: <b>Error<\/b> (JVM problems like OutOfMemoryError; do not catch) and <b>Exception<\/b>. Under Exception, <b>RuntimeException<\/b> and its children are <b>unchecked<\/b>; everything else (IOException, SQLException) is <b>checked<\/b> and must be caught or declared.<\/p>\n<h3>2. try / catch / finally<\/h3><pre class='cd'>try {\n    int n = Integer.parseInt(text);\n    System.out.println(100 / n);\n} catch (NumberFormatException e) {\n    System.out.println(\"Not a number\");\n} catch (ArithmeticException e) {\n    System.out.println(\"Cannot divide by zero\");\n} finally {\n    System.out.println(\"Always runs\");   // even after return\n}<\/pre><ul><li>Order catches from specific to general; a general one first is a compile error.<\/li><li>Multi-catch: <b>catch (A | B e)<\/b>.<\/li><\/ul>\n<h3>3. throw vs throws<\/h3><pre class='cd'>void withdraw(double a) throws InsufficientBalanceException {   // declares\n    if (a &gt; balance) throw new InsufficientBalanceException(\"Short by \" + (a - balance)); // raises\n}<\/pre>\n<h3>4. Custom exception<\/h3><pre class='cd'>class InsufficientBalanceException extends Exception {\n    InsufficientBalanceException(String msg) { super(msg); }\n}<\/pre><p>Extend <b>Exception<\/b> for checked, <b>RuntimeException<\/b> for unchecked.<\/p>\n<h3>5. try-with-resources<\/h3><pre class='cd'>try (BufferedReader br = new BufferedReader(new FileReader(\"a.txt\"))) {\n    System.out.println(br.readLine());\n}   // br.close() called automatically<\/pre>\n<h3>6. Mistakes<\/h3><ul><li>Empty catch blocks hide bugs; at least log e.getMessage().<\/li><li>Catching Exception everywhere.<\/li><li>Putting finally logic that can itself throw.<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\nFN[\"1_1\"]=`<h3>1. Collections framework<\/h3><p>Interfaces: <b>Collection<\/b> splits into <b>List<\/b> (ordered, duplicates allowed), <b>Set<\/b> (unique) and <b>Queue<\/b>. <b>Map<\/b> is separate (key to value).<\/p>\n<h3>2. ArrayList vs LinkedList<\/h3><table><tr><th><\/th><th>ArrayList<\/th><th>LinkedList<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Backed by<\/td><td>Resizable array<\/td><td>Doubly linked nodes<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>get(i)<\/td><td>O(1)<\/td><td>O(n)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Add at end<\/td><td>O(1) amortised<\/td><td>O(1)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Insert/remove middle<\/td><td>O(n) (shifts)<\/td><td>O(1) once positioned, O(n) to find<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Memory<\/td><td>Compact<\/td><td>Extra pointers per node<\/td><\/tr><\/table><p>Default to ArrayList; LinkedList only for frequent head/tail changes.<\/p>\n<h3>3. Core methods<\/h3><pre class='cd'>List&lt;String&gt; names = new ArrayList&lt;&gt;();\nnames.add(\"Asha\"); names.add(0, \"Ravi\");\nnames.get(1); names.set(1, \"Meera\"); names.remove(\"Ravi\");\nnames.contains(\"Asha\"); names.size(); names.isEmpty();\nCollections.sort(names); Collections.reverse(names);\nList&lt;Integer&gt; nums = new ArrayList&lt;&gt;(List.of(5, 1, 7));\nnums.remove(1);                    // by INDEX: removes the element at 1\nnums.remove(Integer.valueOf(7));   // by VALUE<\/pre>\n<h3>4. Iterating<\/h3><pre class='cd'>for (String s : names) System.out.println(s);\nIterator&lt;String&gt; it = names.iterator();\nwhile (it.hasNext()) if (it.next().startsWith(\"A\")) it.remove();   // safe removal<\/pre>\n<h3>5. Worked tasks<\/h3><pre class='cd'>// Remove duplicates, keep order\nList&lt;Integer&gt; u = new ArrayList&lt;&gt;(new LinkedHashSet&lt;&gt;(nums));\n// Merge two sorted lists\nint i = 0, j = 0; List&lt;Integer&gt; m = new ArrayList&lt;&gt;();\nwhile (i &lt; a.size() &amp;&amp; j &lt; b.size()) m.add(a.get(i) &lt;= b.get(j) ? a.get(i++) : b.get(j++));\nwhile (i &lt; a.size()) m.add(a.get(i++));\nwhile (j &lt; b.size()) m.add(b.get(j++));<\/pre>\n<h3>6. Mistakes<\/h3><ul><li>remove(int) vs remove(Object) on List of Integer.<\/li><li>Removing in a for-each loop: ConcurrentModificationException.<\/li><li>List.of(...) is immutable; add() throws UnsupportedOperationException.<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\nFN[\"1_2\"]=`<h3>1. How HashMap works<\/h3><p>For put(key, value): compute <b>key.hashCode()<\/b>, pick a bucket, and inside the bucket use <b>equals<\/b> to find or add the entry. Average get/put is O(1). Two keys in one bucket is a <b>collision<\/b>; Java handles it with a list (and a tree when long). When size passes <b>0.75 &times; capacity<\/b> (load factor), the table doubles and rehashes.<\/p>\n<h3>2. The Map family<\/h3><table><tr><th>Type<\/th><th>Order<\/th><th>get/put<\/th><th>Null key<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>HashMap<\/td><td>None<\/td><td>O(1)<\/td><td>One allowed<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>LinkedHashMap<\/td><td>Insertion<\/td><td>O(1)<\/td><td>One allowed<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>TreeMap<\/td><td>Sorted by key<\/td><td>O(log n)<\/td><td>Not allowed<\/td><\/tr><\/table><p><b>HashSet<\/b> is a HashMap with only keys: unique, unordered. Same idea for LinkedHashSet and TreeSet.<\/p>\n<h3>3. Methods<\/h3><pre class='cd'>Map&lt;String, Integer&gt; m = new HashMap&lt;&gt;();\nm.put(\"a\", 1);  m.get(\"a\");  m.getOrDefault(\"z\", 0);\nm.containsKey(\"a\");  m.remove(\"a\");  m.putIfAbsent(\"b\", 2);\nm.merge(\"a\", 1, Integer::sum);          // count in one line\nfor (Map.Entry&lt;String, Integer&gt; e : m.entrySet())\n    System.out.println(e.getKey() + \"=\" + e.getValue());<\/pre>\n<h3>4. Worked tasks<\/h3><pre class='cd'>// Word frequency\nfor (String w : text.split(\" \")) freq.merge(w, 1, Integer::sum);\n// First non-repeating character\nMap&lt;Character,Integer&gt; c = new LinkedHashMap&lt;&gt;();\nfor (char ch : s.toCharArray()) c.merge(ch, 1, Integer::sum);\nfor (var e : c.entrySet()) if (e.getValue() == 1) { System.out.println(e.getKey()); break; }\n// Two Sum\nMap&lt;Integer,Integer&gt; seen = new HashMap&lt;&gt;();\nfor (int i = 0; i &lt; a.length; i++) {\n    if (seen.containsKey(target - a[i])) return new int[]{seen.get(target - a[i]), i};\n    seen.put(a[i], i);\n}<\/pre>\n<h3>5. equals and hashCode contract<\/h3><p>If a.equals(b) then a.hashCode() == b.hashCode(). Override both together or lookups fail. Never mutate a field used in hashCode after using the object as a key.<\/p>`;\n\nFN[\"1_3\"]=`<h3>1. Generics<\/h3><p>Generics give compile-time type safety and remove casts.<\/p><pre class='cd'>class Pair&lt;K, V&gt; {\n    private K key; private V value;\n    Pair(K k, V v) { key = k; value = v; }\n    K getKey() { return key; }  V getValue() { return value; }\n}\nPair&lt;String,Integer&gt; p = new Pair&lt;&gt;(\"age\", 20);\n\nstatic &lt;T extends Comparable&lt;T&gt;&gt; T max(T a, T b) { return a.compareTo(b) &gt; 0 ? a : b; }<\/pre><ul><li>Only object types: <b>List&lt;Integer&gt;<\/b>, not List&lt;int&gt;.<\/li><li>Type erasure: generics vanish at runtime, so no <b>new T()<\/b>.<\/li><li>Bounded type <b>T extends Number<\/b>; wildcard <b>List&lt;? extends Number&gt;<\/b> for read-only use.<\/li><\/ul>\n<h3>2. Iterator<\/h3><p><b>hasNext()<\/b>, <b>next()<\/b>, <b>remove()<\/b>. Changing the list during for-each throws ConcurrentModificationException; use iterator.remove() or removeIf.<\/p>\n<h3>3. Comparable vs Comparator<\/h3><table><tr><th><\/th><th>Comparable<\/th><th>Comparator<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Method<\/td><td>compareTo(other)<\/td><td>compare(a, b)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Where<\/td><td>Inside the class (natural order)<\/td><td>Outside (any custom order)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Orders<\/td><td>One<\/td><td>Many<\/td><\/tr><\/table><pre class='cd'>class Student implements Comparable&lt;Student&gt; {\n    int marks; String name;\n    public int compareTo(Student o) { return Integer.compare(marks, o.marks); }\n}\nlist.sort(Comparator.comparing(Employee::getSalary).reversed()\n        .thenComparing(Employee::getName));<\/pre><p>compare returns negative if first is smaller, 0 if equal, positive if larger. Use Integer.compare, not subtraction (overflow risk).<\/p>`;\n\nFN[\"1_4\"]=`<h3>1. Lambdas<\/h3><p>A lambda is a short anonymous function for a <b>functional interface<\/b> (exactly one abstract method).<\/p><pre class='cd'>Runnable r = () -&gt; System.out.println(\"run\");\nComparator&lt;String&gt; c = (a, b) -&gt; a.length() - b.length();\nFunction&lt;Integer,Integer&gt; sq = x -&gt; x * x;\nPredicate&lt;Integer&gt; even = x -&gt; x % 2 == 0;\nlist.forEach(System.out::println);      // method reference<\/pre><p>Common interfaces: Function (T to R), Predicate (T to boolean), Consumer (T to nothing), Supplier (nothing to T).<\/p>\n<h3>2. Stream pipeline<\/h3><p><b>Source<\/b> to <b>intermediate<\/b> operations (lazy) to one <b>terminal<\/b> operation (runs it).<\/p><pre class='cd'>List&lt;String&gt; out = names.stream()\n    .filter(n -&gt; n.length() &gt; 3)\n    .map(String::toUpperCase)\n    .sorted()\n    .distinct()\n    .collect(Collectors.toList());\nint total = nums.stream().mapToInt(Integer::intValue).sum();\nlong c = nums.stream().filter(x -&gt; x &gt; 5).count();\nint prod = nums.stream().reduce(1, (a, b) -&gt; a * b);<\/pre>\n<h3>3. Collectors<\/h3><pre class='cd'>Map&lt;String, List&lt;Txn&gt;&gt; byType = txns.stream().collect(Collectors.groupingBy(Txn::getType));\nMap&lt;String, Double&gt; sumByType = txns.stream()\n    .collect(Collectors.groupingBy(Txn::getType, Collectors.summingDouble(Txn::getAmount)));\nString joined = names.stream().collect(Collectors.joining(\", \"));<\/pre>\n<h3>4. Optional<\/h3><pre class='cd'>Optional&lt;Employee&gt; top = emps.stream().max(Comparator.comparingDouble(Employee::getSalary));\ntop.ifPresent(e -&gt; System.out.println(e.getName()));\nString n = Optional.ofNullable(name).orElse(\"Unknown\");<\/pre>\n<h3>5. Mistakes<\/h3><ul><li>A stream can be used only once.<\/li><li>Intermediate operations do nothing without a terminal one.<\/li><li>Changing outside variables inside a lambda (they must be effectively final).<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\nFN[\"1_5\"]=`<h3>1. Text files<\/h3><pre class='cd'>// Write\ntry (BufferedWriter bw = new BufferedWriter(new FileWriter(\"data.txt\", true))) {   // true = append\n    bw.write(\"line one\"); bw.newLine();\n}\n// Read\ntry (BufferedReader br = new BufferedReader(new FileReader(\"data.txt\"))) {\n    String line;\n    while ((line = br.readLine()) != null) System.out.println(line);\n}<\/pre>\n<h3>2. java.nio.file (modern)<\/h3><pre class='cd'>Path p = Path.of(\"data.txt\");\nList&lt;String&gt; lines = Files.readAllLines(p);\nFiles.write(p, List.of(\"a\", \"b\"));\nboolean there = Files.exists(p);<\/pre><p>The File class checks metadata: exists(), length(), delete(), isDirectory().<\/p>\n<h3>3. CSV summary<\/h3><pre class='cd'>double total = 0;\nfor (String l : Files.readAllLines(Path.of(\"sales.csv\")).subList(1, n)) {   // skip header\n    String[] f = l.split(\",\");\n    total += Double.parseDouble(f[2]);\n}<\/pre>\n<h3>4. Serialization<\/h3><pre class='cd'>class Student implements Serializable {\n    String name; transient String password;   // transient is NOT saved\n}\ntry (ObjectOutputStream o = new ObjectOutputStream(new FileOutputStream(\"s.ser\"))) { o.writeObject(s); }\ntry (ObjectInputStream i = new ObjectInputStream(new FileInputStream(\"s.ser\"))) { Student s2 = (Student) i.readObject(); }<\/pre><p>Add <b>serialVersionUID<\/b> to avoid version mismatch errors.<\/p>\n<h3>5. Mistakes<\/h3><ul><li>Not closing streams; use try-with-resources.<\/li><li>Relative path confusion: it resolves from the working directory.<\/li><li>FileNotFoundException and IOException are checked; handle them.<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\nFN[\"1_6\"]=`<h3>1. Creating threads<\/h3><pre class='cd'>class Task implements Runnable {\n    public void run() { System.out.println(Thread.currentThread().getName()); }\n}\nThread t = new Thread(new Task());\nt.start();      // creates a NEW thread\n// t.run();     // just a normal call on the current thread\nt.join();       // wait for it to finish\nThread t2 = new Thread(() -&gt; System.out.println(\"lambda\"));<\/pre><p>Prefer Runnable over extending Thread (a class can extend only one parent).<\/p>\n<h3>2. Lifecycle<\/h3><table><tr><th>State<\/th><th>Meaning<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>NEW<\/td><td>Created, not started<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>RUNNABLE<\/td><td>Ready or running<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>BLOCKED / WAITING<\/td><td>Waiting for a lock or signal<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>TERMINATED<\/td><td>Finished<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>3. Race condition and synchronized<\/h3><pre class='cd'>class Counter {\n    private int c = 0;\n    synchronized void inc() { c++; }   // one thread at a time\n    int get() { return c; }\n}<\/pre><p><b>c++<\/b> is read, add, write: three steps, so two threads can lose updates. With 2 threads &times; 1000 increments, unsynchronised code often prints less than 2000.<\/p>\n<h3>4. ExecutorService<\/h3><pre class='cd'>ExecutorService ex = Executors.newFixedThreadPool(3);\nfor (int i = 0; i &lt; 5; i++) ex.submit(() -&gt; System.out.println(\"task on \" + Thread.currentThread().getName()));\nex.shutdown();\nex.awaitTermination(5, TimeUnit.SECONDS);<\/pre>\n<h3>5. Deadlock<\/h3><p>Thread A holds lock 1 and waits for lock 2, thread B holds lock 2 and waits for lock 1. Avoid it by always taking locks in the same order. Also know <b>volatile<\/b> (visibility of one variable) and <b>AtomicInteger<\/b> (lock-free counter).<\/p>`;\n\nFN[\"1_7\"]=`<h3>1. JDBC flow<\/h3><p>Add the driver jar (MySQL or PostgreSQL) to the project. Then: connect, create statement, execute, read results, close.<\/p><pre class='cd'>String url = \"jdbc:mysql://localhost:3306/bank\";\ntry (Connection con = DriverManager.getConnection(url, \"root\", \"password\");\n     PreparedStatement ps = con.prepareStatement(\"SELECT id, name FROM students WHERE name = ?\")) {\n    ps.setString(1, \"Asha\");\n    try (ResultSet rs = ps.executeQuery()) {\n        while (rs.next()) System.out.println(rs.getInt(\"id\") + \" \" + rs.getString(\"name\"));\n    }\n} catch (SQLException e) { e.printStackTrace(); }<\/pre>\n<h3>2. CRUD<\/h3><pre class='cd'>// INSERT / UPDATE / DELETE use executeUpdate (returns rows affected)\nPreparedStatement ins = con.prepareStatement(\"INSERT INTO students(name, marks) VALUES(?, ?)\");\nins.setString(1, \"Ravi\"); ins.setDouble(2, 88.5);\nint rows = ins.executeUpdate();<\/pre>\n<h3>3. SQL injection<\/h3><p>Bad: <b>\"SELECT * FROM users WHERE name='\" + input + \"'\"<\/b>. Input like <b>' OR '1'='1<\/b> returns every row. A PreparedStatement sends the SQL and the values separately, so input is always treated as data.<\/p>\n<h3>4. Transactions<\/h3><pre class='cd'>con.setAutoCommit(false);\ntry { debit.executeUpdate(); credit.executeUpdate(); con.commit(); }\ncatch (SQLException e) { con.rollback(); }<\/pre><h3>5. Mistakes<\/h3><ul><li>Never hard-code real passwords in code you push to GitHub.<\/li><li>Forgetting that executeQuery is for SELECT only.<\/li><li>Not closing Connection, Statement and ResultSet.<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\nFN[\"1_8\"]=`<h3>1. SOLID<\/h3><table><tr><th>Letter<\/th><th>Rule<\/th><th>Quick example<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>S<\/td><td>A class has one reason to change<\/td><td>Split Invoice (data) from InvoicePrinter<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>O<\/td><td>Open for extension, closed for modification<\/td><td>Add a new Payment class, do not edit a giant if-else<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>L<\/td><td>Subclasses must work wherever the parent is used<\/td><td>A Square that breaks Rectangle's setWidth behaviour violates it<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>I<\/td><td>Many small interfaces beat one fat one<\/td><td>Printer and Scanner, not one MultiFunction for all<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>D<\/td><td>Depend on abstractions, not concrete classes<\/td><td>Service takes a Payment interface, not UPI directly<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>2. Design patterns<\/h3><pre class='cd'>// Singleton (one instance)\nclass Config {\n    private static final Config INSTANCE = new Config();\n    private Config() {}\n    static Config get() { return INSTANCE; }\n}\n// Strategy (swap algorithm at runtime)\ninterface PayStrategy { void pay(double a); }\nclass Checkout { PayStrategy s; Checkout(PayStrategy s) { this.s = s; } void run() { s.pay(500); } }\nnew Checkout(new UpiPay()).run();\n// Factory (create without exposing the class)\nstatic Shape create(String t) { return t.equals(\"circle\") ? new Circle() : new Square(); }<\/pre><table><tr><th>Pattern<\/th><th>Use when<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Builder<\/td><td>Many optional constructor fields<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Observer<\/td><td>Notify many listeners (balance alerts)<\/td><\/tr><\/table><p>Prefer composition (HAS-A) over inheritance. Do not force patterns where simple code works.<\/p>`;\n\nFN[\"1_9\"]=`<h3>1. Mental model<\/h3><p>Git saves snapshots (commits). Working folder, then <b>staging area<\/b> (add), then <b>local repo<\/b> (commit), then <b>remote<\/b> (push).<\/p>\n<h3>2. Commands<\/h3><table><tr><th>Command<\/th><th>Does<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>git init / git clone url<\/td><td>Start or copy a repo<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>git status / git diff<\/td><td>See changes<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>git add . / git commit -m \"msg\"<\/td><td>Stage and save a snapshot<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>git log --oneline<\/td><td>History<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>git branch name / git switch name<\/td><td>Create and move<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>git merge name<\/td><td>Bring a branch in<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>git push origin main / git pull<\/td><td>Upload / download<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>3. .gitignore<\/h3><pre class='cd'>*.class\ntarget/\n.idea/\n*.env<\/pre><p>Never commit passwords, API keys or large binaries.<\/p>\n<h3>4. Good commits and README<\/h3><ul><li>Small commits with messages that say what and why: \"Add withdraw validation\".<\/li><li>README sections: what it is, features, tech stack, how to run, screenshots.<\/li><\/ul>\n<h3>5. First workflow<\/h3><pre class='cd'>git init\ngit add .\ngit commit -m \"Initial ATM project\"\ngit branch -M main\ngit remote add origin https://github.com/you/atm.git\ngit push -u origin main<\/pre>`;\n\nFN[\"1_10\"]=`<h3>Project: Banking Management System (Day 1: design and core)<\/h3>\n<h3>1. Schema<\/h3><pre class='cd'>CREATE TABLE customers (id INT PRIMARY KEY AUTO_INCREMENT, name VARCHAR(50), phone VARCHAR(15));\nCREATE TABLE accounts  (acc_no INT PRIMARY KEY AUTO_INCREMENT, customer_id INT, balance DECIMAL(12,2) DEFAULT 0,\n                        FOREIGN KEY (customer_id) REFERENCES customers(id));\nCREATE TABLE transactions (id INT PRIMARY KEY AUTO_INCREMENT, acc_no INT, type VARCHAR(10), amount DECIMAL(12,2),\n                        created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP, FOREIGN KEY (acc_no) REFERENCES accounts(acc_no));<\/pre>\n<h3>2. Layers<\/h3><table><tr><th>Layer<\/th><th>Classes<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>model<\/td><td>Customer, Account, Transaction<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>dao<\/td><td>AccountDao (JDBC only)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>service<\/td><td>BankService (rules, exceptions)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>app<\/td><td>Main console menu<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>3. Transfer in one transaction<\/h3><pre class='cd'>con.setAutoCommit(false);\ntry {\n    if (balance(from) &lt; amt) throw new InsufficientBalanceException(\"Low balance\");\n    update(from, -amt); update(to, +amt);\n    log(from, \"DEBIT\", amt); log(to, \"CREDIT\", amt);\n    con.commit();\n} catch (Exception e) { con.rollback(); throw e; }<\/pre><p>Both updates succeed or neither does: that is atomicity (the A in ACID).<\/p>\n<h3>4. Day 1 goal<\/h3><ul><li>Schema created, project compiles.<\/li><li>Create account, deposit and withdraw work through JDBC.<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\nFN[\"1_11\"]=`<h3>Project: finish, test, publish (Day 2)<\/h3>\n<h3>1. Remaining features<\/h3><ul><li>Transaction history: <b>SELECT * FROM transactions WHERE acc_no = ? ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 10<\/b>.<\/li><li>Simple login using a stored PIN (store a hash, not plain text).<\/li><li>Handle every bad input; the program must never crash.<\/li><\/ul>\n<h3>2. JUnit tests<\/h3><pre class='cd'>@Test void depositIncreasesBalance() {\n    Account a = new Account(1234, 100);\n    a.deposit(50);\n    assertEquals(150, a.getBalance(), 0.001);\n}\n@Test void withdrawTooMuchThrows() {\n    assertThrows(IllegalArgumentException.class, () -&gt; new Account(1234, 100).withdraw(500));\n}<\/pre><p>Test the happy path, the boundary (exactly the full balance) and the failure case.<\/p>\n<h3>3. Publish<\/h3><ul><li>README: features, tech stack, setup (database script), screenshots.<\/li><li>Keep DB credentials in a config file listed in .gitignore.<\/li><li>Push to GitHub with clear commits.<\/li><\/ul>\n<h3>4. 2-minute explanation (practise aloud)<\/h3><p>Problem (a bank system), design (4 layers, 3 tables), challenge (atomic transfer with rollback), result (tests pass, live repo).<\/p>`;\n\n/* PACK M2-MCQ: hand-made 10-question sets for Month 2 topics 5-12 (streams to project finish). */\nQB[1].push([\n\"A functional interface has exactly how many abstract stream operation actually starts processing?~filter~map~collect~sorted~2~filter is lazy until a terminal call.~map is lazy until a terminal call.~collect is terminal and triggers the pipeline.~sorted is intermediate.\",\n\"What does map(String::length) do?~Filters strings~Turns each string into its length~Counts strings~Sorts strings~1~That is filter.~map transforms every element.~count does that.~sorted does that.\",\n\"Which predicate keeps even numbers?~x -> x % 2 == 0~x -> x / 2~x -> x = 2~x -> x % 2~0~Even numbers leave remainder 0.~Division is not a boolean.~= assigns, it does not compare.~A Predicate must return boolean, not int.\",\n\"What happens if you reuse a consumed stream?~It restarts~IllegalStateException~Returns empty~Nothing~1~Streams cannot restart.~A stream can be consumed only once.~It throws instead of returning empty.~An exception is thrown.\",\n\"Which collector groups items by a key?~Collectors.toList()~Collectors.groupingBy()~Collectors.joining()~Collectors.counting()~1~That only gathers into a list.~groupingBy builds a Map of key to list.~joining builds a String.~counting only counts.\",\n\"What does reduce(0, (a,b) -> a+b) compute on 1,2,3?~3~6~0~123~1~3 is the count.~0+1+2+3 = 6.~0 is only the start value.~It adds, it does not concatenate.\",\n\"Optional is mainly code.~Editing risks old bugs.~final alone does not express it.~Abstract alone does not express it.\",\n\"Which pattern allows exactly one instance?~Factory~Singleton~Observer~Builder~1~Factory creates objects.~Singleton keeps one instance.~Observer notifies listeners.~Builder builds step by step.\",\n\"Which pattern swaps an algorithm at runtime?~Strategy~Singleton~Builder~Adapter~0~Pass a change?~Observer~Singleton~Factory~Prototype~0~Subscribers get updates.~Singleton is one instance.~Factory creates objects.~Prototype clones.\",\n\"Dependency inversion means depend on?~Concrete classes~Abstractions like interfaces~Static methods~Global variables~1~Concrete coupling is the problem.~High-level code uses interfaces.~Static methods hide dependencies.~Globals create hidden coupling.\",\n\"Prefer composition over inheritance because?~It is shorter~It is more flexible and avoids deep hierarchies~Java forbids inheritance~It is faster~1~Length is not the reason.~HAS-A can be swapped at runtime.~Inheritance is allowed.~Speed is similar.\",\n\"Which pattern hides which class is created?~Factory~Singleton~Observer~Iterator~0~The caller asks for a type and gets an object.~Singleton limits instances.~Observer notifies.~Iterator walks collections.\",\n\"Interface segregation prefers?~One big interface~Several small focused interfaces~No interfaces~Only abstract classes~1~Fat interfaces force useless methods.~Clients depend only on what they use.~Interfaces are useful.~Both have uses.\",\n\"Builder pattern is best when?~A class has many optional fields~A only the debit~1~That loses money.~Rolling back keeps the data consistent.~Ignoring corrupts data.~That repeats a half step.\",\n\"Why store a transaction history table?~To audit every movement~To the bank project help to?~Express business errors clearly~Hide errors~Slow code~Replace SQL~0~InsufficientBalanceException tells exactly what failed.~They make errors visible.~They are not for causes it.~Statement with concatenation is unsafe.~Printing does nothing.\"],[\n\"What does a JUnit test check?~That code behaves as expected~That code compiles~That Git works~That the OS is fast~0~Assertions compare expected and actual results.~Compilation is separate.~Git is separate.~Performance is separate.\",\n\"Which assertion checks two values are equal?~assertEquals~assertSame only~assertNone~assertAll~0~assertEquals compares expected and actual.~assertSame checks references.~assertNone does not exist.~assertAll groups assertions.\",\n\"Which JUnit call checks that an exception is thrown?~assertThrows~assertCatch~assertError~assertFail~0~assertThrows runs code and expects an exception type.~assertCatch does not exist.~assertError does not exist.~assertFail forces tools~Nothing~0~This is the interview structure.~Lines are not a story.~Tools alone are not enough.~Prepare content.\",\n\"Why use meaningful commits in the final push?~They show clear progress to
