bad user input?~Handle it without crashing~Crash~Ignore silently~Delete data~0~Validation keeps the app stable.~Crashes look unprofessional.~Silent failure confuses users.~Never delete data.\"]);\n\n/* PACK M3-LESSONS: Month 3 (SQL + DBMS) full lessons. Sample tables used throughout:\n   employees(id, name, dept, salary, manager_id)  customers(id, name, city)  orders(id, customer_id, amount, order_date) */\n\nFN[\"2_0\"]=`<h3>1. Query order of writing<\/h3><pre class='cd'>SELECT   name, salary\nFROM     employees\nWHERE    dept = 'IT' AND salary &gt;= 50000\nORDER BY salary DESC\nLIMIT    5;<\/pre><p>SQL runs it in a different order: <b>FROM, WHERE, GROUP BY, HAVING, SELECT, ORDER BY, LIMIT<\/b>. That is why you cannot use a SELECT alias inside WHERE.<\/p>\n<h3>2. Filters<\/h3><table><tr><th>Operator<\/th><th>Example<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>AND / OR / NOT<\/td><td>dept='IT' OR dept='HR'<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>IN<\/td><td>dept IN ('IT','HR')<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>BETWEEN (inclusive)<\/td><td>salary BETWEEN 40000 AND 60000<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>LIKE<\/td><td>name LIKE 'A%' (starts with A), '%a' (ends), '_s%' (2nd letter s)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>IS NULL<\/td><td>manager_id IS NULL (never = NULL)<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>3. DISTINCT, ORDER BY, LIMIT<\/h3><pre class='cd'>SELECT DISTINCT dept FROM employees;\nSELECT * FROM employees ORDER BY dept ASC, salary DESC;\nSELECT * FROM employees ORDER BY salary DESC LIMIT 3 OFFSET 2;   -- rows 3 to 5<\/pre><p>SQL Server uses <b>TOP 3<\/b>; Oracle uses <b>FETCH FIRST 3 ROWS ONLY<\/b>.<\/p>\n<h3>4. Mistakes<\/h3><ul><li>Writing <b>= NULL<\/b> instead of <b>IS NULL<\/b>.<\/li><li>Mixing AND and OR without brackets.<\/li><li>SELECT * in real queries; name the columns you need.<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\nFN[\"2_1\"]=`<h3>1. Aggregate functions<\/h3><pre class='cd'>SELECT COUNT(*), COUNT(manager_id), SUM(salary), AVG(salary), MIN(salary), MAX(salary) FROM employees;<\/pre><p><b>COUNT(*)<\/b> counts rows; <b>COUNT(col)<\/b> skips NULLs. Aggregates ignore NULLs (except COUNT(*)).<\/p>\n<h3>2. GROUP BY<\/h3><pre class='cd'>SELECT dept, COUNT(*) AS headcount, AVG(salary) AS avg_sal\nFROM employees\nGROUP BY dept;<\/pre><p>Every non-aggregated column in SELECT must appear in GROUP BY.<\/p>\n<h3>3. WHERE vs HAVING<\/h3><table><tr><th>WHERE<\/th><th>HAVING<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Filters rows before grouping<\/td><td>Filters groups after aggregation<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Cannot use aggregates<\/td><td>Can use aggregates<\/td><\/tr><\/table><pre class='cd'>SELECT customer_id, COUNT(*) AS n\nFROM orders\nWHERE order_date &gt;= '2026-01-01'      -- row filter\nGROUP BY customer_id\nHAVING COUNT(*) &gt;= 3;                 -- group filter<\/pre>\n<h3>4. Practice answers<\/h3><pre class='cd'>-- Sales per city\nSELECT c.city, SUM(o.amount) FROM orders o JOIN customers c ON c.id=o.customer_id GROUP BY c.city;\n-- Departments with average salary above 60000\nSELECT dept FROM employees GROUP BY dept HAVING AVG(salary) &gt; 60000;<\/pre>`;\n\nFN[\"2_2\"]=`<h3>1. CASE WHEN<\/h3><pre class='cd'>SELECT name, marks,\n  CASE WHEN marks &gt;= 90 THEN 'A'\n       WHEN marks &gt;= 75 THEN 'B'\n       WHEN marks &gt;= 60 THEN 'C'\n       ELSE 'F' END AS grade\nFROM students;<\/pre><p>First matching branch wins. Also useful inside aggregates: <b>SUM(CASE WHEN status='paid' THEN amount ELSE 0 END)<\/b>.<\/p>\n<h3>2. NULL handling<\/h3><ul><li>Any comparison with NULL is unknown, so use <b>IS NULL<\/b> / <b>IS NOT NULL<\/b>.<\/li><li><b>COALESCE(price, 0)<\/b> returns the first non-NULL value.<\/li><li>NULL + 5 = NULL. COUNT(col) skips NULLs. NOT IN with a NULL in the list returns no rows (a classic trap); prefer NOT EXISTS.<\/li><\/ul>\n<h3>3. String and date functions (MySQL names; others are similar)<\/h3><table><tr><th>Need<\/th><th>Function<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Length, case<\/td><td>LENGTH(s), UPPER(s), LOWER(s)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Join text<\/td><td>CONCAT(a, ' ', b)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Cut text<\/td><td>SUBSTRING(s, 1, 3), TRIM(s), REPLACE(s, 'a', 'b')<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Year / month<\/td><td>YEAR(d), MONTH(d), DATE_FORMAT(d, '%Y-%m')<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Difference<\/td><td>DATEDIFF(d1, d2), DATE_ADD(d, INTERVAL 7 DAY)<\/td><\/tr><\/table><pre class='cd'>SELECT YEAR(order_date) y, MONTH(order_date) m, SUM(amount) FROM orders GROUP BY y, m;<\/pre>`;\n\nFN[\"2_3\"]=`<h3>1. Join types<\/h3><table><tr><th>Join<\/th><th>Returns<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>INNER<\/td><td>Only rows that match in both tables<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>LEFT<\/td><td>All left rows; right columns NULL if no match<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>RIGHT<\/td><td>All right rows; left columns NULL if no match<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>FULL<\/td><td>All rows from both (not in MySQL; use LEFT UNION RIGHT)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>CROSS<\/td><td>Every combination (m x n rows)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>SELF<\/td><td>A table joined to itself using aliases<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>2. Examples<\/h3><pre class='cd'>-- Customers with their orders\nSELECT c.name, o.amount FROM customers c INNER JOIN orders o ON o.customer_id = c.id;\n\n-- Customers who never ordered (LEFT JOIN + IS NULL)\nSELECT c.name FROM customers c LEFT JOIN orders o ON o.customer_id = c.id WHERE o.id IS NULL;\n\n-- Employee and manager (self join)\nSELECT e.name AS employee, m.name AS manager\nFROM employees e LEFT JOIN employees m ON e.manager_id = m.id;\n\n-- Three tables\nSELECT c.name, p.title FROM orders o\nJOIN customers c ON c.id = o.customer_id\nJOIN products  p ON p.id = o.product_id;<\/pre>\n<h3>3. Rules<\/h3><ul><li>Always write the ON condition; forgetting it creates a cross join.<\/li><li>A WHERE filter on the right table of a LEFT JOIN turns it into an inner join; put such conditions in ON.<\/li><li>Draw two overlapping circles and shade the result before writing any join.<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\nFN[\"2_4\"]=`<h3>1. Subquery kinds<\/h3><pre class='cd'>-- Scalar: returns one value\nSELECT name FROM employees WHERE salary &gt; (SELECT AVG(salary) FROM employees);\n\n-- List: used with IN\nSELECT name FROM customers WHERE id IN (SELECT customer_id FROM orders WHERE amount &gt; 1000);\n\n-- Correlated: refers to the outer row; runs per outer row\nSELECT e.name FROM employees e\nWHERE e.salary &gt; (SELECT AVG(salary) FROM employees WHERE dept = e.dept);\n\n-- Derived table (in FROM, needs an alias)\nSELECT d.dept, d.avg_sal FROM (SELECT dept, AVG(salary) avg_sal FROM employees GROUP BY dept) d WHERE d.avg_sal &gt; 60000;<\/pre>\n<h3>2. IN vs EXISTS<\/h3><table><tr><th>IN<\/th><th>EXISTS<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Compares against a list of values<\/td><td>Checks whether any row exists<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>NOT IN breaks if the list has NULL<\/td><td>NOT EXISTS handles NULLs safely<\/td><\/tr><\/table><pre class='cd'>SELECT c.name FROM customers c\nWHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM orders o WHERE o.customer_id = c.id);<\/pre><h3>3. Tip<\/h3><p>Many correlated subqueries can be rewritten as joins or window functions and are faster. Start with the subquery for clarity, then optimise.<\/p>`;\n\nFN[\"2_5\"]=`<h3>1. CTE basics<\/h3><p>A Common Table Expression is a named temporary result for one query. It makes long SQL readable.<\/p><pre class='cd'>WITH dept_avg AS (\n    SELECT dept, AVG(salary) AS avg_sal FROM employees GROUP BY dept\n)\nSELECT e.name, e.salary, d.avg_sal\nFROM employees e JOIN dept_avg d ON d.dept = e.dept\nWHERE e.salary &gt; d.avg_sal;<\/pre>\n<h3>2. Multiple CTEs<\/h3><pre class='cd'>WITH big AS (SELECT * FROM orders WHERE amount &gt; 1000),\n     per_cust AS (SELECT customer_id, COUNT(*) n FROM big GROUP BY customer_id)\nSELECT * FROM per_cust WHERE n &gt;= 2;<\/pre><p>Later CTEs can use earlier ones. Separate them with commas; only one WITH keyword.<\/p>\n<h3>3. Recursive CTE (org chart)<\/h3><pre class='cd'>WITH RECURSIVE chain AS (\n    SELECT id, name, manager_id, 1 AS lvl FROM employees WHERE manager_id IS NULL   -- anchor\n    UNION ALL\n    SELECT e.id, e.name, e.manager_id, c.lvl + 1\n    FROM employees e JOIN chain c ON e.manager_id = c.id                            -- recursive step\n)\nSELECT * FROM chain ORDER BY lvl;<\/pre><p>Anchor query starts it, the recursive part repeats until no new rows appear.<\/p>`;\n\nFN[\"2_6\"]=`<h3>1. Window function shape<\/h3><p>A window function calculates over related rows <b>without collapsing them<\/b> (unlike GROUP BY).<\/p><pre class='cd'>function() OVER (PARTITION BY dept ORDER BY salary DESC)<\/pre>\n<h3>2. Ranking<\/h3><table><tr><th>Salary<\/th><th>ROW_NUMBER<\/th><th>RANK<\/th><th>DENSE_RANK<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>90<\/td><td>1<\/td><td>1<\/td><td>1<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>90<\/td><td>2<\/td><td>1<\/td><td>1<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>80<\/td><td>3<\/td><td>3<\/td><td>2<\/td><\/tr><\/table><p>ROW_NUMBER never ties; RANK ties and skips; DENSE_RANK ties without skipping.<\/p><pre class='cd'>-- Top 3 salaries per department\nSELECT * FROM (\n  SELECT name, dept, salary, DENSE_RANK() OVER (PARTITION BY dept ORDER BY salary DESC) rk FROM employees\n) t WHERE rk &lt;= 3;<\/pre>\n<h3>3. LAG, LEAD, running total<\/h3><pre class='cd'>SELECT month, revenue,\n  LAG(revenue)  OVER (ORDER BY month) AS prev_month,\n  revenue - LAG(revenue) OVER (ORDER BY month) AS change,\n  SUM(revenue)  OVER (ORDER BY month) AS running_total\nFROM monthly_sales;<\/pre><p>You cannot filter on a window function in WHERE; wrap the query in a subquery or CTE first.<\/p>`;\n\nFN[\"2_7\"]=`<h3>1. Keys<\/h3><table><tr><th>Key<\/th><th>Meaning<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Super key<\/td><td>Any set of columns that uniquely identifies a row<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Candidate key<\/td><td>A minimal super key (no extra column)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Primary key<\/td><td>The chosen candidate key; unique and NOT NULL<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Alternate key<\/td><td>A candidate key not chosen as primary<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Foreign key<\/td><td>A column referencing another table's key<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Composite key<\/td><td>A key made of several columns<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>2. Constraints<\/h3><pre class='cd'>CREATE TABLE students (\n  id INT PRIMARY KEY,\n  email VARCHAR(80) UNIQUE NOT NULL,\n  age INT CHECK (age &gt;= 16),\n  dept_id INT DEFAULT 1,\n  FOREIGN KEY (dept_id) REFERENCES departments(id) ON DELETE CASCADE\n);<\/pre>\n<h3>3. ER diagrams<\/h3><ul><li><b>Entity<\/b> = rectangle (Student), <b>attribute<\/b> = oval, <b>relationship<\/b> = diamond (Enrolls).<\/li><li>Cardinality: 1:1, 1:N (Customer has many Orders), M:N (Student and Course).<\/li><li>An M:N relationship becomes a <b>junction table<\/b> with two foreign keys: enrollments(student_id, course_id).<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\nFN[\"2_8\"]=`<h3>1. Why normalise<\/h3><p>Normalisation removes redundancy and three anomalies: <b>insert<\/b> (cannot add data without other data), <b>update<\/b> (change in many rows), <b>delete<\/b> (losing unrelated facts).<\/p>\n<h3>2. Normal forms<\/h3><table><tr><th>Form<\/th><th>Rule<\/th><th>Fix<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>1NF<\/td><td>Atomic values, no repeating groups<\/td><td>Split \"phones: 1,2\" into rows<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>2NF<\/td><td>1NF + no partial dependency on part of a composite key<\/td><td>Move attributes that depend on one key part to their own table<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>3NF<\/td><td>2NF + no transitive dependency (non-key depends on non-key)<\/td><td>Move city-to-state style facts out<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>BCNF<\/td><td>Every determinant is a candidate key<\/td><td>Split where a non-key determines part of a key<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>3. Worked example<\/h3><p>Orders(order_id, product_id, product_name, customer_id, customer_city). product_name depends only on product_id (partial) and customer_city only on customer_id.<\/p><pre class='cd'>Orders(order_id, product_id, customer_id)\nProducts(product_id, product_name)\nCustomers(customer_id, customer_city)<\/pre><p><b>Functional dependency<\/b> A to B means A determines B. Denormalise on purpose only for read-heavy reporting.<\/p>`;\n\nFN[\"2_9\"]=`<h3>1. ACID<\/h3><table><tr><th>Letter<\/th><th>Meaning<\/th><th>Bank example<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Atomicity<\/td><td>All or nothing<\/td><td>Debit and credit both happen or neither<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Consistency<\/td><td>Rules always hold<\/td><td>Total money unchanged<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Isolation<\/td><td>Concurrent transactions do not interfere<\/td><td>Two withdrawals do not corrupt balance<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Durability<\/td><td>Committed data survives a crash<\/td><td>Saved to disk or log<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>2. Transaction commands<\/h3><pre class='cd'>START TRANSACTION;\nUPDATE accounts SET balance = balance - 500 WHERE id = 1;\nUPDATE accounts SET balance = balance + 500 WHERE id = 2;\nCOMMIT;      -- or ROLLBACK; to undo<\/pre>\n<h3>3. Concurrency problems<\/h3><table><tr><th>Problem<\/th><th>Meaning<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Dirty read<\/td><td>Reading uncommitted data<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Non-repeatable read<\/td><td>Same row read twice, different value<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Phantom read<\/td><td>Same query returns new rows<\/td><\/tr><\/table><table><tr><th>Isolation level<\/th><th>Prevents<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Read Uncommitted<\/td><td>Nothing<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Read Committed<\/td><td>Dirty reads<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Repeatable Read<\/td><td>Dirty + non-repeatable<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Serializable<\/td><td>All three (slowest)<\/td><\/tr><\/table><p><b>Deadlock:<\/b> two transactions each wait for the other's lock; the DB aborts one.<\/p>`;\n\nFN[\"2_10\"]=`<h3>1. Indexes<\/h3><p>An index is a sorted structure (usually a <b>B-tree<\/b>) that lets the database find rows without scanning the whole table.<\/p><pre class='cd'>CREATE INDEX idx_emp_dept ON employees(dept);\nCREATE INDEX idx_orders_cust_date ON orders(customer_id, order_date);   -- composite\nEXPLAIN SELECT * FROM employees WHERE dept = 'IT';<\/pre><table><tr><th>Clustered<\/th><th>Non-clustered<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Defines physical row order; one per table (often the primary key)<\/td><td>Separate structure pointing to rows; many allowed<\/td><\/tr><\/table><ul><li>Helps WHERE, JOIN, ORDER BY on the indexed column.<\/li><li>Costs storage and slows INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE.<\/li><li>A function on the column (<b>WHERE YEAR(d)=2026<\/b>) or a leading wildcard (<b>LIKE '%x'<\/b>) prevents index use.<\/li><li>Composite index follows the leftmost-prefix rule.<\/li><\/ul>\n<h3>2. Views<\/h3><pre class='cd'>CREATE VIEW it_staff AS SELECT id, name, salary FROM employees WHERE dept = 'IT';\nSELECT * FROM it_staff;<\/pre><p>A view is a saved query: simplifies access and hides columns. It stores no data (unless materialised).<\/p>\n<h3>3. Procedures and triggers<\/h3><ul><li><b>Stored procedure:<\/b> saved SQL you CALL with parameters.<\/li><li><b>Trigger:<\/b> runs automatically on INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE (for example, write an audit row).<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\nFN[\"2_11\"]=`<h3>Classic SQL interview set<\/h3>\n<h3>1. Nth highest salary<\/h3><pre class='cd'>SELECT DISTINCT salary FROM (\n  SELECT salary, DENSE_RANK() OVER (ORDER BY salary DESC) rk FROM employees\n) t WHERE rk = 3;<\/pre>\n<h3>2. Find duplicates<\/h3><pre class='cd'>SELECT email, COUNT(*) FROM users GROUP BY email HAVING COUNT(*) &gt; 1;<\/pre>\n<h3>3. Employees earning more than their manager<\/h3><pre class='cd'>SELECT e.name FROM employees e JOIN employees m ON e.manager_id = m.id WHERE e.salary &gt; m.salary;<\/pre>\n<h3>4. Top N per group<\/h3><pre class='cd'>SELECT * FROM (SELECT *, ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY dept ORDER BY salary DESC) rn FROM employees) t WHERE rn &lt;= 2;<\/pre>\n<h3>5. Consecutive-day logins<\/h3><pre class='cd'>SELECT DISTINCT user_id FROM (\n  SELECT user_id, login_date,\n         DATE_SUB(login_date, INTERVAL ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY user_id ORDER BY login_date) DAY) AS grp\n  FROM (SELECT DISTINCT user_id, login_date FROM logins) x\n) y GROUP BY user_id, grp HAVING COUNT(*) &gt;= 3;<\/pre><p>Idea: subtracting the row number from the date gives the same value for a run of consecutive days.<\/p>\n<h3>6. Method for any SQL question<\/h3><ul><li>State the output columns and the grain (one row per what?).<\/li><li>Choose the tables and the join keys.<\/li><li>Filter (WHERE), group (GROUP BY), filter groups (HAVING), then rank or limit.<\/li><li>Check NULLs, duplicates and ties.<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\n/* PACK M3-MCQ: hand-made 10-question sets for Month 3 (SQL + DBMS). */\nQB[2]=[[\n\"Which clause filters rows before grouping?~HAVING~WHERE~ORDER BY~LIMIT~1~HAVING filters groups.~WHERE filters individual rows.~ORDER BY sorts.~LIMIT caps the row count.\",\n\"How do you test for a missing value?~= NULL~IS NULL~== NULL~LIKE NULL~1~Nothing equals NULL.~IS NULL is the correct test.~== is not SQL.~LIKE is for patterns.\",\n\"What does BETWEEN 10 AND 20 include?~10 and 20~Only values between~Only 20~Only 10~0~Both ends are included.~Ends are included too.~It also includes 10.~It also includes 20.\",\n\"Which pattern finds names starting with A?~LIKE '%A'~LIKE 'A%'~LIKE '_A'~= 'A'~1~That ends with A.~% after A allows any text after it.~_ means exactly one character before A.~= needs the exact whole value.\",\n\"Which removes duplicate rows from output?~UNIQUE~DISTINCT~DIFFERENT~GROUP~1~UNIQUE is a constraint.~DISTINCT removes repeats.~DIFFERENT is not SQL.~GROUP BY groups, it is not the keyword here.\",\n\"Default sort direction of ORDER BY is?~DESC~ASC~Random~By id~1~DESC must be written.~Ascending is the default.~It is deterministic.~It sorts by the named column.\",\n\"Which returns rows 3 to 5 in MySQL?~LIMIT 3 OFFSET 2~LIMIT 5~TOP 3~SKIP 2~0~Skips 2 rows then returns 3.~That returns the first 5.~TOP is SQL Server.~SKIP is not MySQL.\",\n\"Which runs first?~SELECT~FROM~ORDER BY~HAVING~1~SELECT runs late.~FROM picks the tables first.~ORDER BY is near the end.~HAVING runs after grouping.\",\n\"What does IN ('IT','HR') mean?~Between IT and HR~Equal to IT or HR~Contains IT and HR~Not IT~1~It is not a range.~It matches any value in the list.~It is not a substring test.~That would be NOT IN.\",\n\"Why use AND with OR carefully?~Precedence: AND runs before OR~OR is slower~They are the same~SQL forbids it~0~Use brackets to be explicit.~Speed is not the issue.~They differ in precedence.~It is allowed.\"],[\n\"What does COUNT(*) count?~Non-NULL values~All rows~Distinct values~Columns~1~That is COUNT(column).~It counts every row.~That needs DISTINCT.~It counts rows.\",\n\"Which function ignores NULLs?~COUNT(*)~AVG(salary)~Both~Neither~1~COUNT(*) counts all rows.~AVG skips NULL values.~Only one of these.~AVG ignores NULLs.\",\n\"Every non-aggregated SELECT column must be in?~WHERE~GROUP BY~HAVING~LIMIT~1~WHERE is for filtering.~Columns must be grouped.~HAVING filters groups.~LIMIT caps rows.\",\n\"HAVING is used to?~Filter groups~Filter rows~Join tables~Sort~0~It works on aggregated results.~That is WHERE.~Joins use JOIN.~Sorting uses ORDER BY.\",\n\"Which query is valid?~WHERE COUNT(*) > 3~HAVING COUNT(*) > 3~WHERE AVG(x) > 1~SELECT HAVING~1~Aggregates are not allowed in WHERE.~HAVING can use aggregates.~WHERE cannot hold AVG.~Not valid syntax.\",\n\"AVG of 10, 20, NULL is?~10~15~20~NULL~1~NULL is skipped, not zero.~(10+20)/2 = 15.~That is the max.~It is not NULL.\",\n\"What does GROUP BY dept return?~One row per dept~All rows~One row total~Nothing~0~Rows are collapsed per group.~Without grouping all rows appear.~Only with no GROUP BY and an aggregate.~It returns groups.\",\n\"Which finds customers with 3 or more orders?~GROUP BY customer_id HAVING COUNT(*) >= 3~WHERE COUNT(*) >= 3~ORDER BY 3~LIMIT 3~0~Group then filter groups.~Aggregates are not allowed in WHERE.~That sorts.~That caps rows.\",\n\"MAX(salary) returns?~Highest salary value~Name of the top earner~Row count~Average~0~It returns the value only.~You need another query for the name.~COUNT gives that.~AVG gives that.\",\n\"SUM(amount) on an empty table gives?~0~NULL~Error~Blank text~1~0 would need COALESCE.~SUM of no rows is NULL.~It does not error.~It is not text.\"],[\n\"In CASE WHEN, which branch runs?~Every true branch~The first true branch~The last true branch~Random~1~Only one result is returned.~The first matching branch wins.~The first, not the last.~It is ordered.\",\n\"What does COALESCE(a, 0) do?~Adds zero~Returns a, or 0 if a is NULL~Deletes NULL~Sorts~1~It does not add.~It returns the first non-NULL value.~It does not delete.~It does not sort.\",\n\"NULL + 5 equals?~5~NULL~0~Error~1~NULL propagates.~Any math with NULL gives NULL.~It is not zero.~It does not error.\",\n\"Why is NOT IN risky?~It is slow~A NULL in the list makes it return no rows~It forbids numbers~It needs an index~1~Speed is not the main issue.~Comparisons with NULL are unknown.~Numbers are fine.~Indexes are optional.\",\n\"Which safer alternative handles NULLs?~NOT EXISTS~NOT LIKE~NOT BETWEEN~NOT TOP~0~It checks row existence.~LIKE is for patterns.~BETWEEN is for ranges.~TOP is not a filter.\",\n\"Which gets the year of a date in MySQL?~YEAR(d)~GETYEAR(d)~DATE(d, y)~YEARS(d)~0~YEAR returns the year part.~GETYEAR is not MySQL.~Wrong signature.~YEARS does not exist.\",\n\"CONCAT('a','b') returns?~ab~a b~a,b~NULL~0~It joins text directly.~No space is added.~No comma is added.~Not NULL.\",\n\"TRIM(' x ') returns?~x~ x~ x ~Error~0~It removes spaces at both ends.~Leading space is removed too.~Trailing space is removed too.~It works.\",\n\"Which is true about NULL and COUNT(col)?~COUNT(col) counts NULLs~COUNT(col) skips NULLs~It errors~It counts blanks~1~Only COUNT(*) counts rows.~NULL values are skipped.~No error.~Blank text is not NULL.\",\n\"DATEDIFF(d1, d2) in MySQL returns?~Days between dates~Months~Years~A date~0~It gives a day count.~Not months.~Not years.~Not a date.\"],[\n\"Which join keeps all left rows?~INNER~LEFT~CROSS~SELF~1~INNER keeps matches only.~LEFT keeps every left row.~CROSS pairs all.~SELF is a table with itself.\",\n\"What does INNER JOIN return?~Matching rows in both~All left rows~All rows~Only unmatched~0~Only rows with a match both sides.~That is LEFT.~That is FULL.~That is a LEFT JOIN with IS NULL.\",\n\"How do you find customers with no orders?~LEFT JOIN then WHERE o.id IS NULL~INNER JOIN~CROSS JOIN~RIGHT JOIN only~0~Unmatched rows have NULL on the right.~INNER drops them.~CROSS makes all pairs.~RIGHT keeps the wrong side.\",\n\"A CROSS JOIN of 3 and 4 rows gives?~7~12~3~4~1~It multiplies.~3 x 4 = 12.~Only the left count.~Only the right count.\",\n\"A self join needs?~Aliases~Two databases~A view~An index~0~Alias each copy of the table.~One database is enough.~Views are optional.~Indexes are optional.\",\n\"Forgetting the ON condition causes?~A cross join~A syntax error always~Faster query~NULL results~0~Rows are combined with all rows.~Some databases accept it silently.~It gets slower.~Rows are not NULL.\",\n\"A WHERE filter on the right table of a LEFT JOIN?~Can turn it into an inner join~Is always safe~Adds rows~Deletes the table~0~NULL rows fail the filter.~It can remove unmatched rows.~It cannot add rows.~Tables are untouched.\",\n\"Which is not natively in MySQL?~INNER~LEFT~FULL OUTER JOIN~CROSS~2~INNER exists.~LEFT exists.~Use LEFT UNION RIGHT instead.~CROSS exists.\",\n\"Employee and manager names use?~Self join on manager_id~Cross join~Union~Group by~0~Join employees to employees.~Cross gives all pairs.~Union stacks rows.~Group collapses rows.\",\n\"RIGHT JOIN keeps?~All right rows~All left rows~Only matches~Nothing~0~Unmatched left becomes NULL.~That is LEFT.~That is INNER.~It returns rows.\"],[\n\"A scalar subquery returns?~One value~Many rows~A table~Nothing~0~One row, one column.~That is a list subquery.~That is a derived table.~It must return something.\",\n\"A correlated subquery?~Uses a column from the outer query~Runs once~Has no WHERE~Is always faster~0~It is re-evaluated per outer row.~It runs per row, not once.~It usually has WHERE.~It is often slower.\",\n\"A subquery in FROM needs?~An alias~An index~A trigger~LIMIT~0~Derived tables must be named.~Indexes are optional.~Triggers are unrelated.~LIMIT is optional.\",\n\"EXISTS returns?~Boolean true or false~A number~A table~NULL always~0~It checks row presence.~It does not count.~It does not return rows.~It is true or false.\",\n\"Which finds employees above their department average?~Correlated subquery on dept~SELECT *~ORDER BY dept~UNION~0~Compare to the same dept average.~Nothing compares.~Sorting does not filter.~UNION stacks results.\",\n\"IN with a subquery checks?~Membership in the result list~Row existence only~Sorting~Indexes~0~Value is in the list.~That is EXISTS.~No sorting.~No indexes.\",\n\"Why does NOT EXISTS beat NOT IN?~Safe with NULLs~It is shorter~It needs no table~It is shorter always~0~NOT IN fails with NULLs.~Length is not the reason.~It needs a subquery.~Not always shorter.\",\n\"SELECT 1 inside EXISTS is?~Fine; the value is ignored~Required to be 1~An error~Counted~0~Only existence matters.~Any value works.~It is valid.~It is not counted.\",\n\"A scalar subquery with two rows causes?~An error~Success~NULL~Sorting~0~It must return one row.~It cannot succeed.~It does not give NULL.~Nothing sorts.\",\n\"Many correlated subqueries can be rewritten as?~Joins or window functions~Triggers~Indexes~Views only~0~Often faster.~Triggers are unrelated.~Indexes are separate.~Views are not the rewrite.\"],[\n\"WITH creates?~A CTE~An index~A table on disk~A trigger~0~A named temporary result.~Indexes are separate.~CTE is not stored.~Triggers are separate.\",\n\"A CTE lasts for?~One statement~The whole session~Forever~One day~0~Only the query that follows.~It is not session wide.~It is not permanent.~No time limit.\",\n\"Multiple CTEs are separated by?~Commas~Semicolons~UNION~AND~0~One WITH, then commas.~Semicolons end the statement.~UNION stacks results.~AND is a filter.\",\n\"A recursive CTE has?~An anchor and a recursive step~Only a loop~No union~A trigger~0~Start query plus repeat step.~SQL has no loops here.~It uses UNION ALL.~Triggers are unrelated.\",\n\"Recursive CTEs suit?~Hierarchies like org charts~Sorting~Averages~Deleting~0~Walk parent to child levels.~ORDER BY sorts.~AVG averages.~DELETE deletes.\",\n\"Which keyword starts recursion in MySQL?~WITH RECURSIVE~LOOP~REPEAT~CYCLE~0~That is the syntax.~LOOP is for procedures.~REPEAT is for procedures.~CYCLE is not used.\",\n\"CTEs mainly improve?~Readability~Disk space~Security~Network~0~They name steps.~They store nothing.~They do not secure.~They do not touch network.\",\n\"A later CTE can use?~An earlier CTE~Nothing~Only tables~Only views~0~They can chain.~They can reference earlier ones.~Tables are also allowed.~Views are allowed but not the only option.\",\n\"UNION ALL in a recursive CTE?~Combines anchor and recursive rows~Removes duplicates~Sorts~Drops rows~0~It appends rows.~UNION removes duplicates.~It does not sort.~It does not drop.\",\n\"A recursive CTE stops when?~No new rows are produced~After 10 rows~Never~On commit~0~It ends at an empty step.~There is no fixed row limit.~It does terminate.~Commits are unrelated.\"],[\n\"Window functions differ from GROUP BY because?~They keep every row~They drop rows~They sort only~They delete~0~Rows are not collapsed.~They do not drop rows.~They calculate.~They do not delete.\",\n\"Which gives no ties?~ROW_NUMBER~RANK~DENSE_RANK~Both RANK and DENSE_RANK~0~Each row gets a unique number.~RANK ties.~DENSE_RANK ties.~Both can tie.\",\n\"Scores 90, 90, 80: RANK gives?~1,1,2~1,1,3~1,2,3~1,2,2~1~That is DENSE_RANK.~RANK skips after a tie.~That is ROW_NUMBER.~Not RANK.\",\n\"Scores 90, 90, 80: DENSE_RANK gives?~1,1,2~1,1,3~1,2,3~2,2,3~0~No gaps after a tie.~That is RANK.~That is ROW_NUMBER.~Ranks start at 1.\",\n\"PARTITION BY does what?~Restarts the calculation per group~Sorts globally~Deletes~Joins~0~Each partition is separate.~ORDER BY sorts.~It does not delete.~It does not join.\",\n\"LAG(x) returns?~The previous row value~The next row value~The max~The count~0~It looks back.~That is LEAD.~MAX is a different function.~COUNT is different.\",\n\"Running total uses?~SUM() OVER (ORDER BY ...)~SUM with GROUP BY~COUNT(*)~RANK()~0~A window sum accumulates.~GROUP BY collapses.~COUNT counts.~RANK ranks.\",\n\"Why wrap a window result in a subquery to filter?~WHERE runs before windows are computed~Windows are slow~SQL forbids it~Subqueries are required~0~Windows are evaluated after WHERE.~Speed is not the reason.~It is allowed with a wrapper.~Only for filtering windows.\",\n\"Top 3 per department best uses?~DENSE_RANK over PARTITION BY dept~LIMIT 3~GROUP BY~UNION~0~Rank within each dept.~LIMIT is global.~Group collapses.~UNION stacks.\",\n\"LEAD(x) returns?~The next row value~The previous~The sum~Nothing~0~It looks ahead.~That is LAG.~SUM is different.~It returns a value.\"],[\n\"Which key is a minimal unique identifier?~Candidate key~Super key~Foreign key~Composite only~0~Minimal means no extra column.~Super may have extras.~Foreign references another table.~Composite is about multiple columns.\",\n\"A primary key can contain NULL?~Yes~No~Sometimes~Only one NULL~1~It is NOT NULL.~It must always have a value.~Never.~Not even one.\",\n\"A foreign key?~References another table's key~Is always unique~Is a super key~Stores indexes~0~It links tables.~It may repeat.~Not necessarily.~It stores values.\",\n\"An M:N relationship becomes?~A junction table~One column~A view~A trigger~0~Two foreign keys in a new table.~One column cannot hold it.~Views are queries.~Triggers are + Aptitude). Defines fr(n,d) to show stacked fractions. */\nconst fr=(n,d)=>`<span style=\"display:inline-block;text-align:center;vertical-align:middle;margin:0 3px\"><span style=\"display:block;border-bottom:1px solid currentColor;padding:0 4px\">${n}<\/span><span style=\"display:block;padding:0 4px\">${d}<\/span><\/span>`;\n\nFN[\"3_0\"]=`<h3>1. Python vs Java<\/h3><table><tr><th><\/th><th>Java<\/th><th>Python<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Types<\/td><td>Declared: int x = 5;<\/td><td>Dynamic: x = 5<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Blocks<\/td><td>Braces { }<\/td><td>Indentation (4 spaces)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Line end<\/td><td>Semicolon<\/td><td>Newline<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Run<\/td><td>Compile then run<\/td><td>Interpreted: python file.py<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>2. Basics<\/h3><pre class='cd'>name = input(\"Name: \")           # always a str\nage = int(input(\"Age: \"))\nprice = 99.5; ok = True; nothing = None\nprint(f\"{name} is {age}\")        # f-string\nprint(7 / 2, 7 // 2, 7 % 2, 2 ** 10)   # 3.5 3 1 1024<\/pre><p>Types: int (unlimited size), float, str, bool, NoneType. Check with <b>type(x)<\/b>. <b>/<\/b> always gives a float; <b>//<\/b> is floor division.<\/p>\n<h3>3. Conditions and loops<\/h3><pre class='cd'>if marks &gt;= 90: g = \"A\"\nelif marks &gt;= 60: g = \"B\"\nelse: g = \"C\"\n\nfor i in range(1, 6): print(i)          # 1..5 (end excluded)\nfor i in range(10, 0, -2): print(i)     # 10 8 6 4 2\nn = 5\nwhile n &gt; 0: n -= 1\nfor ch in \"abc\": print(ch)\nfor i, ch in enumerate(\"abc\"): print(i, ch)<\/pre><p>Also: <b>and, or, not<\/b> instead of &amp;&amp; || !; <b>break<\/b>, <b>continue<\/b> and <b>pass<\/b> (do nothing); chained comparison <b>1 &lt; x &lt; 10<\/b>.<\/p>\n<h3>4. Strings<\/h3><pre class='cd'>s = \"Python\"\ns[0], s[-1], s[1:4], s[::-1]      # P n yth nohtyP\ns.upper(); s.split(\",\"); \" \".join([\"a\",\"b\"]); s.replace(\"P\",\"J\"); s.find(\"t\")<\/pre><h3>5. Mistakes<\/h3><ul><li>Mixing tabs and spaces causes IndentationError.<\/li><li>input() returns text; convert with int() before maths.<\/li><li>range(5) stops at 4.<\/li><\/ul>`;\n\nFN[\"3_1\"]=`<h3>1. The four collections<\/h3><table><tr><th>Type<\/th><th>Syntax<\/th><th>Ordered<\/th><th>Mutable<\/th><th>Duplicates<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>list<\/td><td>[1, 2]<\/td><td>Yes<\/td><td>Yes<\/td><td>Yes<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>tuple<\/td><td>(1, 2)<\/td><td>Yes<\/td><td>No<\/td><td>Yes<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>set<\/td><td>{1, 2}<\/td><td>No<\/td><td>Yes<\/td><td>No<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>dict<\/td><td>{\"a\": 1}<\/td><td>Insertion order<\/td><td>Yes<\/td><td>Keys unique<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>2. Lists<\/h3><pre class='cd'>a = [5, 2, 9]\na.append(1); a.insert(0, 7); a.pop(); a.remove(2); a.sort(); a.reverse()\na[1:3]; a[::-1]; len(a); 9 in a; sorted(a, reverse=True)\nb = a[:]       # copy; b = a would share the same list<\/pre>\n<h3>3. Sets and dicts<\/h3><pre class='cd'>s = {1, 2, 3}; t = {3, 4}\ns | t; s &amp; t; s - t; s ^ t        # union, intersection, difference, symmetric diff\nunique = list(set([1,1,2]))\n\nd = {\"a\": 1}\nd[\"b\"] = 2; d.get(\"z\", 0); d.pop(\"a\"); \"b\" in d\nfor k, v in d.items(): print(k, v)\nfrom collections import Counter\nCounter(\"banana\")                 # a:3, n:2, b:1<\/pre><p>Dict and set keys must be <b>hashable<\/b> (immutable): numbers, strings, tuples; not lists.<\/p>\n<h3>4. Tasks<\/h3><pre class='cd'>words = \"to be or not to be\".split()\nfreq = {}\nfor w in words: freq[w] = freq.get(w, 0) + 1\n\nseen = set(); out = []\nfor x in data:\n    if x not in seen: seen.add(x); out.append(x)     # dedupe, keep order<\/pre>`;\n\nFN[\"3_2\"]=`<h3>1. Functions<\/h3><pre class='cd'>def area(l, b=1):             # default argument\n    return l * b\narea(3); area(b=2, l=4)       # keyword arguments\n\ndef total(*nums):             # *args: any number of positional\n    return sum(nums)\ndef info(**kw):               # **kwargs: any keyword arguments\n    for k, v in kw.items(): print(k, v)<\/pre><p>No return means the function returns None. Never use a mutable default like <b>def f(x=[])<\/b>; the list is shared between calls.<\/p>\n<h3>2. Comprehensions<\/h3><pre class='cd'>squares = [x * x for x in range(1, 6)]            # [1, 4, 9, 16, 25]\nevens   = [x for x in nums if x % 2 == 0]\npairs   = {w: len(w) for w in words}               # dict comprehension\nunique  = {x % 3 for x in nums}                    # set comprehension<\/pre>\n<h3>3. lambda, map, filter, sorted<\/h3><pre class='cd'>sq = lambda x: x * x\nlist(map(lambda x: x * 2, [1, 2, 3]))        # [2, 4, 6]\nlist(filter(lambda x: x &gt; 1, [1, 2, 3]))     # [2, 3]\npeople.sort(key=lambda p: (p[\"age\"], p[\"name\"]))\nsorted(words, key=len, reverse=True)<\/pre><h3>4. Scope<\/h3><p>LEGB rule: Local, Enclosing, Global, Built-in. Use <b>global x<\/b> only when you must assign a global from inside a function.<\/p>`;\n\nFN[\"3_3\"]=`<h3>1. Classes<\/h3><pre class='cd'>class BankAccount:\n    bank = \"ABC\"                       # class variable (shared)\n    def __init__(self, owner, balance=0):\n        self.owner = owner             # instance variable\n        self._balance = balance        # _ = \"private by convention\"\n    def deposit(self, amt):\n        if amt &lt;= 0: raise ValueError(\"Amount must be positive\")\n        self._balance += amt\n    def __str__(self): return f\"{self.owner}: {self._balance}\"\nacc = BankAccount(\"Asha\", 100); acc.deposit(50); print(acc)<\/pre><p><b>self<\/b> is the current object (like this in Java) and must be the first parameter of instance methods.<\/p>\n<h3>2. Inheritance<\/h3><pre class='cd'>class Savings(BankAccount):\n    def __init__(self, owner, balance, rate):\n        super().__init__(owner, balance)\n        self.rate = rate\n    def add_interest(self): self.deposit(self._balance * self.rate)<\/pre><p>Python allows multiple inheritance. Methods are overridden by defining the same name; there is no overloading, use default arguments instead.<\/p>\n<h3>3. Exceptions<\/h3><pre class='cd'>class InsufficientFunds(Exception): pass\ntry:\n    x = int(\"abc\")\nexcept ValueError as e:\n    print(\"Bad number\", e)\nexcept (ZeroDivisionError, TypeError):\n    print(\"Math or type problem\")\nelse:\n    print(\"No error\")          # runs only if no exception\nfinally:\n    print(\"Always\")\nwith open(\"a.txt\") as f: data = f.read()      # auto-closes, like try-with-resources<\/pre><h3>4. Java comparison<\/h3><table><tr><th>Java<\/th><th>Python<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>this<\/td><td>self (explicit)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>private<\/td><td>_name convention<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>toString()<\/td><td>__str__()<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>throw / catch<\/td><td>raise / except<\/td><\/tr><\/table>`;\n\nFN[\"3_4\"]=`<h3>1. Tools for DSA in Python<\/h3><pre class='cd'>from collections import deque, Counter, defaultdict\nimport heapq, bisect\n\nq = deque([1, 2]); q.append(3); q.popleft()       # O(1) both ends\ng = defaultdict(list); g[\"a\"].append(\"b\")          # no KeyError\nc = Counter(nums); c.most_common(2)                 # top-2 frequent\n\nh = []; heapq.heappush(h, 5); heapq.heappush(h, 1); heapq.heappop(h)   # min-heap: 1\nheapq.nlargest(3, nums); heapq.nsmallest(3, nums)\n# max-heap trick: push negatives\n\narr = [1, 3, 5, 7]\nbisect.bisect_left(arr, 5)     # 2: index where 5 fits (binary search)<\/pre>\n<h3>2. Sorting<\/h3><pre class='cd'>nums.sort(); sorted(nums, key=lambda x: -x)\nintervals.sort(key=lambda p: p[0])<\/pre><p>Sort is Timsort, O(n log n), and stable.<\/p>\n<h3>3. Worked tasks<\/h3><pre class='cd'># Top-K frequent\n[x for x, _ in Counter(nums).most_common(k)]\n# Merge intervals\nres = []\nfor s, e in sorted(intervals):\n    if res and s &lt;= res[-1][1]: res[-1][1] = max(res[-1][1], e)\n    else: res.append([s, e])\n# Sliding window maximum with deque of indexes\ndq = deque(); out = []\nfor i, x in enumerate(a):\n    while dq and a[dq[-1]] &lt;= x: dq.pop()\n    dq.append(i)\n    if dq[0] &lt;= i - k: dq.popleft()\n    if i &gt;= k - 1: out.append(a[dq[0]])<\/pre><p>Common costs: list append O(1), list insert(0) O(n), set/dict lookup O(1) average, <b>x in list<\/b> O(n).<\/p>`;\n\nFN[\"3_5\"]=`<h3>1. Core objects<\/h3><pre class='cd'>import pandas as pd\ndf = pd.read_csv(\"sales.csv\")\ndf.head(); df.info(); df.describe(); df.shape; df.columns\ns = df[\"amount\"]                       # Series (one column)<\/pre>\n<h3>2. Selecting and filtering<\/h3><pre class='cd'>df[[\"city\", \"amount\"]]\ndf[df[\"amount\"] &gt; 1000]\ndf[(df[\"city\"] == \"Pune\") &amp; (df[\"amount\"] &gt; 500)]       # use &amp; | ~ with brackets\ndf.loc[0:5, \"city\"]        # by label;  df.iloc[0:5, 0:2] by position<\/pre>\n<h3>3. Cleaning<\/h3><pre class='cd'>df.isna().sum()                         # missing per column\ndf[\"amount\"] = df[\"amount\"].fillna(df[\"amount\"].median())\ndf = df.dropna(subset=[\"city\"]).drop_duplicates()\ndf[\"date\"] = pd.to_datetime(df[\"date\"])\ndf[\"month\"] = df[\"date\"].dt.month\ndf[\"city\"] = df[\"city\"].str.strip().str.title()<\/pre>\n<h3>4. Aggregation (SQL in Python)<\/h3><pre class='cd'>df.groupby(\"city\")[\"amount\"].sum().sort_values(ascending=False)\ndf.groupby(\"city\").agg(total=(\"amount\", \"sum\"), orders=(\"id\", \"count\"))\norders.merge(customers, left_on=\"customer_id\", right_on=\"id\", how=\"left\")   # LEFT JOIN\npd.pivot_table(df, index=\"city\", columns=\"month\", values=\"amount\", aggfunc=\"sum\")<\/pre><table><tr><th>SQL<\/th><th>pandas<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>WHERE<\/td><td>boolean mask<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>GROUP BY<\/td><td>groupby<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>JOIN<\/td><td>merge<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>ORDER BY<\/td><td>sort_values<\/td><\/tr><\/table><p>Habit: after loading any dataset run head, info, describe and an isna count before analysing.<\/p>`;\n\nFN[\"3_6\"]=`<h3>1. Percentages<\/h3><p><b>Formula (percentage):<\/b> p% of N = ${fr(\"p\",\"100\")} &times; N<\/p><table><tr><th>Fraction<\/th><th>Percent<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>${fr(\"1\",\"2\")}<\/td><td>50%<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>${fr(\"1\",\"3\")}<\/td><td>33.33%<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>${fr(\"1\",\"4\")}<\/td><td>25%<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>${fr(\"1\",\"5\")}<\/td><td>20%<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>${fr(\"1\",\"8\")}<\/td><td>12.5%<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>${fr(\"1\",\"10\")}<\/td><td>10%<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<p><b>Formula (percentage change):<\/b> % change = ${fr(\"New &minus; Old\",\"Old\")} &times; 100<\/p><p>Successive change a% then b%: net = a + b + ${fr(\"a &times; b\",\"100\")}<\/p><p><b>Example.<\/b> A price rises 20% then falls 20%.<br>Step 1, <i>successive change formula<\/i>: net = 20 + (&minus;20) + ${fr(\"20 &times; (&minus;20)\",\"100\")} = &minus;4<br>Answer: a net decrease of 4%.<\/p>\n<h3>2. Profit and loss<\/h3><p><b>Formula (profit %):<\/b> Profit % = ${fr(\"SP &minus; CP\",\"CP\")} &times; 100 &nbsp; <b>Formula (SP):<\/b> SP = CP &times; ${fr(\"100 + P%\",\"100\")}<\/p><p><b>Example.<\/b> CP = 800, SP = 920.<br>Step 1, profit = SP &minus; CP = 120.<br>Step 2, <i>profit % formula<\/i>: ${fr(\"120\",\"800\")} &times; 100 = 15%.<\/p>\n<p><b>Discount:<\/b> Discount % = ${fr(\"MP &minus; SP\",\"MP\")} &times; 100. Two successive discounts a% and b% equal one discount of a + b &minus; ${fr(\"ab\",\"100\")}.<\/p>\n<h3>3. Ratio and proportion<\/h3><p>If a : b = 3 : 5 and the total is 160, then a = ${fr(\"3\",\"8\")} &times; 160 = 60 and b = ${fr(\"5\",\"8\")} &times; 160 = 100. <b>Formula (sharing):<\/b> share = ${fr(\"part\",\"sum of parts\")} &times; total.<\/p><p>Compound ratios multiply: (a : b) and (b : c) give a : c by making b equal. Direct proportion: x &prop; y means ${fr(\"x\",\"y\")} is constant.<\/p><h3>4. Speed tip<\/h3><p>Convert to a base of 100 (assume the original is 100). 15% of 80 = 10% of 80 + 5% of 80 = 8 + 4 = 12.<\/p>`;\n\nFN[\"3_7\"]=`<h3>1. Time and work<\/h3><p><b>Formula (rate):<\/b> work per day = ${fr(\"1\",\"days\")}<\/p><p><b>Formula (together):<\/b> days = ${fr(\"A &times; B\",\"A + B\")} for two people taking A and B days.<\/p><p><b>Example.<\/b> A finishes in 12 days, B in 18 days.<br>Step 1, <i>together formula<\/i>: ${fr(\"12 &times; 18\",\"12 + 18\")} = ${fr(\"216\",\"30\")} = 7.2 days.<\/p><p>Pipes: inlet is positive work, outlet is negative. Efficiency method: take LCM of the days as total work. LCM(12, 18) = 36 units; A does 3 units/day, B does 2, together 5, so 36 &divide; 5 = 7.2 days.<\/p>\n<h3>2. Speed, distance, time<\/h3><p><b>Formula:<\/b> Speed = ${fr(\"Distance\",\"Time\")}. Convert: km/h &times; ${fr(\"5\",\"18\")} = m/s.<\/p><p><b>Average speed<\/b> for equal distances: ${fr(\"2xy\",\"x + y\")}.<\/p><p><b>Relative speed:<\/b> opposite directions add the speeds, same direction subtract.<\/p><p><b>Example.<\/b> A 150 m train crosses a pole in 10 s.<br>Speed = ${fr(\"150\",\"10\")} = 15 m/s. For a 250 m platform: time = ${fr(\"150 + 250\",\"15\")} = 26.67 s (train length plus platform).<\/p><p>Boats: downstream = u + v, upstream = u &minus; v; still-water speed = ${fr(\"down + up\",\"2\")}.<\/p>\n<h3>3. Averages<\/h3><p><b>Formula:<\/b> Average = ${fr(\"Sum of values\",\"Number of values\")}. If a new value changes the mean of n items by d, then new value = old mean + n &times; d.<\/p><p><b>Weighted average:<\/b> ${fr(\"n&#8321;a&#8321; + n&#8322;a&#8322;\",\"n&#8321; + n&#8322;\")}.<\/p>\n<h3>4. Mixtures and alligation<\/h3><p>Mix cheaper (c) and dearer (d) to get a mean price m: ${fr(\"Quantity cheaper\",\"Quantity dearer\")} = ${fr(\"d &minus; m\",\"m &minus; c\")}.<\/p><p><b>Example.<\/b> Rs 30 and Rs 50 per kg mixed to get Rs 42.<br>Ratio = ${fr(\"50 &minus; 42\",\"42 &minus; 30\")} = ${fr(\"8\",\"12\")} = 2 : 3 (cheaper : dearer).<\/p>`;\n\nFN[\"3_8\"]=`<h3>1. Counting principles<\/h3><p><b>Multiplication rule:<\/b> independent steps multiply. 3 shirts &times; 4 trousers = 12 outfits. <b>Addition rule:<\/b> either/or cases add.<\/p>\n<h3>2. Permutations and combinations<\/h3><p><b>Formula (permutation, order matters):<\/b> nPr = ${fr(\"n!\",\"(n &minus; r)!\")}<\/p><p><b>Formula (combination, order does not matter):<\/b> nCr = ${fr(\"n!\",\"r! (n &minus; r)!\")}, and nCr = nC(n&minus;r).<\/p><p><b>Example.<\/b> Choose a team of 3 from 8.<br>Step 1, <i>combination formula<\/i>: 8C3 = ${fr(\"8 &times; 7 &times; 6\",\"3 &times; 2 &times; 1\")} = ${fr(\"336\",\"6\")} = 56.<\/p>\n<table><tr><th>Case<\/th><th>Count<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Arrange n different items in a row<\/td><td>n!<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Arrange in a circle<\/td><td>(n &minus; 1)!<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Letters with repeats (p, q alike)<\/td><td>${fr(\"n!\",\"p! q!\")}<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Two items always together<\/td><td>(n &minus; 1)! &times; 2!<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>3. Probability<\/h3><p><b>Formula:<\/b> P(E) = ${fr(\"Favourable outcomes\",\"Total outcomes\")}, with 0 &le; P &le; 1.<\/p><p>P(not E) = 1 &minus; P(E). P(A or B) = P(A) + P(B) &minus; P(A and B). Independent events: P(A and B) = P(A) &times; P(B).<\/p><p><b>Example.<\/b> Two dice sum to 7.<br>Favourable: (1,6) (2,5) (3,4) (4,3) (5,2) (6,1) = 6 of 36.<br>P = ${fr(\"6\",\"36\")} = ${fr(\"1\",\"6\")}.<\/p><p><b>Cards:<\/b> 52 cards, 4 suits of 13; 4 kings, 12 face cards, 26 red cards. <b>Coins:<\/b> n coins give 2<sup>n<\/sup> outcomes.<\/p>`;\n\nFN[\"3_9\"]=`<h3>1. Series<\/h3><p>Check these in order: constant difference (arithmetic); constant ratio (geometric); differences that themselves form a pattern; squares or cubes; primes; two interleaved series; alternating operations.<\/p><table><tr><th>Series<\/th><th>Rule<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>2, 6, 12, 20, 30<\/td><td>n(n + 1): differences 4, 6, 8, 10<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>3, 6, 12, 24<\/td><td>&times; 2<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>1, 4, 9, 16<\/td><td>squares<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>1, 1, 2, 3, 5, 8<\/td><td>sum of previous two<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>2. Blood relations<\/h3><p>Draw a family tree. Use a letter for each person, + for male and &minus; for female, a line for parent-child and = for married. Read the question one clause at a time.<\/p><p><b>Example.<\/b> \"Pointing to a man, Riya says, his mother is my mother's only daughter.\" The mother's only daughter is Riya herself, so the man's mother is Riya: he is her son.<\/p>\n<h3>3. Directions<\/h3><p>Plot on a grid: north up, east right. Final distance uses the <b>Pythagoras theorem:<\/b> d = &radic;(x&sup2; + y&sup2;). Example: 3 km north then 4 km east gives d = &radic;(9 + 16) = 5 km north-east of the start.<\/p>\n<h3>4. Seating<\/h3><ul><li>Linear: write positions 1 to n and place fixed facts first.<\/li><li>Circular facing the centre: left of a person is clockwise-right on your diagram; facing outward reverses it.<\/li><li>Write each clue as a small sketch, then combine and eliminate.<\/li><\/ul><h3>5. Method<\/h3><p>Time-box each puzzle to 2 minutes; if stuck, skip and return. Use a table for attribute puzzles (person, city, colour).<\/p>`;\n\nFN[\"3_10\"]=`<h3>1. Reading comprehension<\/h3><ul><li>Read the questions first, then the passage once, noting the main idea per paragraph.<\/li><li>Answer from the text only, not from outside knowledge.<\/li><li>Eliminate options that are too extreme (always, never, only) or true-but-not-stated.<\/li><li>Tone and inference questions: look for the author's choice of words.<\/li><\/ul>\n<h3>2. Grammar rules tested most<\/h3><table><tr><th>Rule<\/th><th>Wrong<\/th><th>Right<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Subject-verb agreement<\/td><td>The list of items are ready<\/td><td>The list of items is ready (subject is list)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Each / every / either / neither<\/td><td>Each of the boys have<\/td><td>Each of the boys has<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Tense consistency<\/td><td>He said he will come<\/td><td>He said he would come<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Pronoun agreement<\/td><td>Everyone brought their own<\/td><td>Everyone brought his or her own (formal tests)<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Parallel structure<\/td><td>She likes reading and to swim<\/td><td>She likes reading and swimming<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Less / fewer<\/td><td>fewer water<\/td><td>less water, fewer bottles<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>3. Sentence correction method<\/h3><ul><li>Find the verb and its subject first.<\/li><li>Check tense, then pronouns, then modifiers, then punctuation.<\/li><li>Choose the shortest correct option that keeps the meaning.<\/li><\/ul>\n<h3>4. Vocabulary<\/h3><p>Learn words in pairs (synonym and antonym) from context. Use roots: <b>bene<\/b> good, <b>mal<\/b> bad, <b>dict<\/b> speak, <b>port<\/b> carry. Read business news daily to build placement-test vocabulary.<\/p>`;\n\nFN[\"3_11\"]=`<h3>Timed aptitude mock (60 minutes)<\/h3>\n<h3>1. Paper plan<\/h3><table><tr><th>Section<\/th><th>Questions<\/th><th>Time<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Quantitative<\/td><td>20<\/td><td>25 min<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Logical reasoning<\/td><td>15<\/td><td>20 min<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Verbal<\/td><td>15<\/td><td>15 min<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>2. Rules<\/h3><ul><li>One sitting, no phone, no calculator unless your target tests allow it.<\/li><li>First pass: answer every question you can do in under 90 seconds. Mark the rest.<\/li><li>Second pass: attempt marked questions by order of confidence.<\/li><li>Leave the last 3 minutes for checking calculations.<\/li><\/ul>\n<h3>3. Analysis (the real learning)<\/h3><p>For every wrong or skipped question, write one line: topic | why missed (concept, silly error, time) | the formula or trick.<\/p><table><tr><th>Cause<\/th><th>Fix<\/th><\/tr><tr><td>Concept gap<\/td><td>Re-read the lesson and do 5 more questions on it<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Calculation slip<\/td><td>Practise tables to 20 and squares to 30<\/td><\/tr><tr><td>Time pressure<\/td><td>Learn to skip; aim for 1 minute per quant question<\/td><\/tr><\/table>\n<h3>4. Score target<\/h3><p>Aim for 70 percent accuracy at full pace by Month 9. Track the score in your daily notes box.<\/p>`;\n\n/* PACK M4-MCQ: hand-made sets for Month 4 (Python + Aptitude). Aptitude answers are numerically verified. */\nQB[3]=[[\n\"What is 7 // 2 in Python?~3.5~3~4~2~1~/ gives 3.5, not //.~// is floor division, so 3.~It does not round up.~2 is the remainder part of nothing here.\",\n\"What type does input() return?~int~str~float~bool~1~You must convert to int yourself.~input always returns text.~Convert with float().~bool is not returned.\",\n\"range(1, 5) produces?~1 2 3 4 5~1 2 3 4~0 1 2 3 4~2 3 4 5~1~The end value 5 is excluded.~It starts at 1 and stops before 5.~That is range(5).~It starts at 1, not 2.\",\n\"How does Python mark a code block?~Braces~Indentation~begin/end~Semicolons~1~Braces are Java.~Indentation defines blocks.~begin/end are not Python.~Semicolons are optional.\",\n\"What is 'abc'[::-1]?~abc~cba~bca~error~1~That is the original.~Step -1 reverses the string.~That is not a reversal.~It is valid slicing.\",\n\"What is 2 ** 3?~6~8~9~5~1~6 would be 2 * 3.~** is power: 2 cubed is 8.~9 is 3 squared.~5 would be 2 + 3.\",\n\"Which is correct f-string syntax?~f'{name}'~'{name}'~f(name)~format name~0~The f prefix and braces insert the value.~Without f it prints the braces.~f(name) is a function call.~Not logical AND operator?~&&~and~&~AND~1~&& is Java.~Python uses the word and.~& is bitwise.~Keywords are lowercase.\",\n\"How many values does range(10, 0, -3) give?~3~4~5~10~1~It gives 10, 7, 4, 1, which is more than 3.~10, 7, 4, 1 is four values.~Not five.~Not ten.\"],[\n\"Which is immutable?~list~tuple~set~dict~1~Lists can change.~Tuples cannot change.~Sets can change.~Dicts can change.\",\n\"len({1, 2, 2, 3}) is?~4~3~2~1~1~Duplicates are removed.~The set holds 1, 2, 3.~Three distinct values remain.~Not one.\",\n\"d.get('z', 0) returns what if z is missing?~KeyError~0~None~Empty~1~get does not raise.~The default 0 is returned.~None only if no default is given.~Not empty.\",\n\"b = a for a list a means?~An independent copy~Both names share one list~A tuple~A new set~1~Use a[:] to copy.~Assignment copies the reference only.~It stays a list.~It stays a list.\",\n\"Which operator gives the set union?~&~|~-~^~1~& is intersection.~| is union.~- is difference.~^ is symmetric difference.\",\n\"Which can be a dict key?~[1, 2]~(1, 2)~{1, 2}~{'a': 1}~1~Lists are unhashable.~Tuples are hashable.~Sets are unhashable.~Dicts are unhashable.\",\n\"[10, 20, 30, 40][1:3] is?~[10, 20]~[20, 30]~[20, 30, 40]~[10, 20, 30]~1~Index 0 is excluded.~Start 1 up to but excluding 3.~Index 3 is excluded.~Index 0 is excluded.\",\n\"Counter('aab').most_common(1) gives?~[('a', 2)]~[('b', 1)]~['a']~2~1~b appears once.~a occurs twice.~It returns pairs.~Counter returns pairs, not the number.\",\n\"Which removes duplicates but loses order for a list?~sorted~set~reversed~len~1~Sorting keeps duplicates.~A set stores unique values unordered.~Reversing keeps duplicates.~len counts.\",\n\"x in a_list costs about?~O(1)~O(n)~O(log n)~O(n^2)~1~That is a set or dict.~It scans the list.~That needs sorted data.~Not quadratic.\"],[\n\"What type is args inside def f(*args)?~list~tuple~dict~set~1~It is not a list.~*args collects into a tuple.~**kwargs is a dict.~It is not a set.\",\n\"[x * x for x in range(1, 4)] is?~[1, 2, 3]~[1, 4, 9]~[1, 4, 9, 16]~[2, 4, 6]~1~Those are the inputs.~1, 4, 9 are the reason.~The same list persists across calls.~It is valid syntax.~They can be, which is the problem.\",\n\"(lambda x: x + 1)(3) returns?~3~4~x + 1~Error~1~It adds 1.~3 + 1 = 4.~It is evaluated.~It is valid.\",\n\"A function with no return statement returns?~0~None~Empty string~False~1~Not zero.~It returns None.~Not a string.~Not False.\",\n\"map(f, xs) returns?~A list~An iterator~A tuple~A dict~1~Use list(map(...)) for a list.~It is a lazy iterator.~It is not a tuple.~It is not a dict.\",\n\"sorted(words, key=len) sorts by?~Alphabet~Word length~Last letter~Random~1~That is the dict.~Not a list.~Not a set.\",\n\"In LEGB, L stands for?~Loop~Local~Library~Lambda~1~Loop is not a scope.~Local scope is searched first.~Library is not a scope.~Lambda is not the L.\",\n\"How many elements in {x % 3 for x in [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6]}?~6~3~2~1~1~Duplicates collapse in a set.~Values 1, 2, 0 give 3 distinct.~There are three remainders.~More than one.\"],[\n\"Which method is the Python object~The parent~A module~1~The class is accessed by name.~It is the instance being used.~super() refers to the parent.~Modules are separate.\",\n\"super().__init__(...) does what?~Deletes the when?~An exception occurs~No exception occurs~Always~Never~1~That is except.~It runs only if try succeeded.~finally always runs.~It does run.\",\n\"with open('a.txt') as f: ensures?~Faster reads~The returns readable text.~hashCode is __hash__.~main is the entry.\",\n\"_balance with a leading underscore means?~Enforced private~Private by convention~Constant~Static~1~Python does not enforce it.~It signals internal use.~Constants use UPPER_CASE.~Static is different.\",\n\"Python method overloading is done with?~Same name, different parameters~Default arguments~Interfaces~Not possible at all~1~Only the last definition survives.~Default arguments and *args cover it.~Interfaces are not required.~It is emulated.\",\n\"Multiple inheritance in Python is?~Not allowed~Allowed~Only with smallest.~Deterministic.\",\n\"Python sort is?~Quick sort, unstable~Timsort, stable~Bubble sort~Heap sort~1~It is not plain quicksort.~Timsort is stable and O(n log n).~Not bubble sort.~Not heap Selling price?~900~990~1100~800~0~1000 x 90/100 = 900.~That is a 1% discount.~That is a mark-up.~That is 20%.\",\n\"Two successive discounts 10% and 20% equal one discount of?~30%~28%~25%~32%~1~They do not simply add.~10 + 20 - (10 x 20)/100 = 28.~Not 25%.~Not 32%.\",\n\"Increase from 40 to 50 is what percent?~10%~20%~25%~50%~2~10 is the difference, not the percent.~Not 20%.~(10/40) x 100 = 25%.~Not 50%.\",\n\"a : b = 2 : 3 and b : c = 4 : 5. Then a : c is?~2 : 5~8 : 15~6 : 20~4 : 15~1~Not obtained by dropping b.~Make b equal: 8 : 12 : 15 gives 8 : 15.~Not 6 : 20.~Not 4 : 15.\"],[\n\"A does a job in 10 days, B in 15. Together?~5~6~7.5~25~1~Faster than that.~(10 x 15)/(10 + 15) = 6 days.~Not the average.~That is the sum.\",\n\"72 km/h in m/s is?~10~20~25~36~1~That is 36 km/h.~72 x 5/18 = 20.~Not 25.~Not 36.\",\n\"Average speed for equal distances at 40 and 60 km/h?~50~48~45~52~1~Not the simple mean.~2 x 40 x 60/(40 + 60) = 48.~Not 45.~Not 52.\",\n\"A 100 m train at 10 m/s crosses a pole in?~1 s~10 s~100 s~20 s~1~Too fast.~Time = distance/speed = 100/10.~Not 100.~Not 20.\",\n\"Boat 10 km/h still water, stream 2 km/h. Downstream speed?~8~12~10~20~1~That is upstream.~10 + 2 = 12.~That ignores the stream.~Too high.\",\n\"Mean of 4, 8, 12 is?~6~8~10~12~1~Not 6.~(4 + 8 + 12)/3 = 8.~Not 10.~That is the largest.\",\n\"Average of 5 numbers is 20. Cheaper : dearer is?~2 : 3~3 : 2~1 : 1~4 : 6~1~That would be the reverse.~Alligation: (30 - 24) : (24 - 20) = 6 : 4 = 3 : 2.~Not equal parts.~Equals 2 : 3, which is the reverse.\",\n\"Pipe A fills in 6 h, pipe B empties in 12 h. Relative speed?~20~100~50~2400~1~That is for the same direction.~Opposite directions add: 100.~Not the average.~Not a product.\"],[\n\"How many ways to arrange 5 different books in a row?~25~120~60~24~1~That is 5 x 5.~5! = 120.~Not 60.~That is 4!.\",\n\"5C2 equals?~20~10~25~5~1~That is 5P2.~5!/(2! 3!) = 10.~Not 25.~Not 5.\",\n\"4P2 equals?~6~12~8~16~1~That is 4C2.~4!/2! = 12.~Not 8.~Not 16.\",\n\"Two coin tosses. P(both heads)?~1/2~1/4~1/3~3/4~1~Too high.~1/2 x 1/2 = 1/4.~Not 1/3.~That is at least one head.\",\n\"A fair die is rolled. P(even number)?~1/6~1/2~1/3~2/3~1~That is one face.~3 of 6 faces.~That is two faces.~Not 2/3.\",\n\"Arrangements of the letters in LEVEL?~120~30~60~24~1~Repeats reduce it.~5!/(2! x 2!) = 30.~Not 60.~Not 24.\",\n\"Circular arrangements of 5 people?~120~24~60~5~1~That is a row.~(5 - 1)! = 24.~Not 60.~Not 5.\",\n\"P(king) from a 52-card deck?~1/52~1/13~1/4~4/13~1~That is one specific card.~4/52 = 1/13.~That is a suit.~That is 16 cards.\",\n\"If P(E) = 0.3, P(not E) is?~0.3~0.7~1.3~0~1~Not equal.~1 - 0.3 = 0.7.~Probability cannot exceed 1.~Not zero.\",\n\"10C3 equals?~30~120~720~210~1~Not 30.~10 x 9 x 8/(3 x 2 x 1) = 120.~That is 10P3 = 720.~Not 210.\"],[\n\"Next in 2, 4, 8, 16, ?~24~32~30~20~1~Not +8.~Each term doubles.~Not 30.~Not 20.\",\n\"Next in 1, 4, 9, 16, ?~20~25~24~36~1~Not +4.~Squares: 5 squared is 25.~Not 24.~That is 6 squared.\",\n\"Next in 3, 6, 12, 24, ?~36~48~30~42~1~Not +12.~Each term doubles.~Not 30.~Not 42.\",\n\"Next in 1, 1, 2, 3, 5, ?~6~8~7~9~1~Not 6.~Sum of previous two: 3 + 5 = 8.~Not 7.~Not 9.\",\n\"Next in 2, 6, 12, 20, 30, ?~40~42~44~36~1~Differences are 4, 6, 8, 10, 12.~30 + 12 = 42.~Not 44.~Not 36.\",\n\"Pointing to a photo Ravi says: she is the daughter of my mother's only son. You face?~West~East~South~North~1~Left turn gives west.~Right of north is east.~That is a U-turn.~No change.\",\n\"All cats are animals; all animals breathe. Then?~All cats breathe~Some animals are not cats~No cat breathes~Nothing follows~0~Valid syllogism.~True but not the conclusion.~Contradicts.~Something follows.\",\n\"Next in 1, 2, 6, 24, 120, ?~240~720~600~360~1~Not 240.~Multiply by 6: 120 x 6 = 720.~Not 600.~Not 360.\"],[\n\"The list of items ___ ready.~are~is~were~be~1~The subject is list.~Singular subject takes is.~Past tense does not fit.~Not grammatical.\",\n\"Each of the boys ___ a ticket.~have~has~are~had been~1~Each is singular.~Each takes has.~Not are.~Not this form.\",\n\"___ bottles were left.~Less~Fewer~Much~Little~1~Less forms.~Wrong forms.\",\n\"He said he ___ come.~will~would~shall~is~1~Reported speech shifts tense.~would matches said.~Not shall.~Not is.\",\n\"In reading comprehension, avoid options that are?~Stated
